- DS-000 (design system tooling check) built and moved to review, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `packages/ui` now holds shadcn/ui 4.23.0 on Base UI 1.9.0 (`base-nova` style, `components.json`) with React 19.3.1, Tailwind CSS 4.3.3 through `@tailwindcss/vite`, and the eleven components of ADR 0130 (shadcn/ui on Base UI with Tailwind CSS v4): button, toggle-group, switch, popover, dialog, dropdown-menu, tabs, slider, select, combobox and tooltip, plus toggle, input, textarea and input-group, which the registry added as their dependencies. `packages/tokens` gained `tokens.css` with first-pass kit colours for dark (default) and light (`data-theme="light"`) from the mock's token block, a 4 px space unit and radii 4, 7 and 10, and `theme.css`, which maps the kit names and shadcn's names into `@theme inline` and drops Tailwind's default palette. These values are a first pass that the round 6 freeze refreshes, and DS-001 (design tokens package) is not ticked. Storybook 10.6.5 in `packages/ui` has 30 stories with a dark and light toolbar, the a11y addon, `storybook` and `build-storybook` scripts, and React Compiler through the package's `vite.config.ts` as ADR 0016 (React 19 SPA with React Compiler) sets. `test:browser` builds the static Storybook and runs axe-core 4.14.0 through Playwright over every story in both themes, so the CI `browser` job runs it. The first axe run found 16 failing story and theme pairs, all fixed: link buttons and inactive tabs now use kit colours that pass contrast, slider thumbs take `getAriaLabel`, and the combobox's chevron button has a name and leaves the tab order. Base UI's focus guard spans are excluded from axe ([result](results/2026-11-01-axe-over-the-component-stories.md), outcome written into ADR 0130). `scripts/adopt.ts` strips comments with oxc-parser and renames the shadcn classes that clash with the kit's `accent`, and a unit test fails if a component or story still has either. Lint config changed in three places: Biome parses Tailwind directives, three Biome rules are off only in `packages/ui/src/components` for patterns shadcn uses on purpose, and `noLabelWithoutControl` knows `Switch`. Surprising: shadcn now ships `cn` from its own `cn` package instead of clsx and tailwind-merge, Storybook's a11y addon runs its own axe pass that collides with a second one unless the URL sets `a11y.manual`, and plugin-react 6.1.2 accepts `@rolldown/plugin-babel` only up to 0.2.x. Two of 19 local axe runs had test timeouts that did not reproduce, so the suite retries once in CI. Lessons are in [knowledge/frontend-ui.md](knowledge/frontend-ui.md). No cloud resources were used.
- STACK-007 (component primitives and styling) decided by the owner in chat: shadcn/ui on Base UI with Tailwind CSS v4 and the kit's tokens in `@theme`, recorded as [ADR 0130](adr/0130-shadcn-ui-on-base-ui-with-tailwind-v4.md) (shadcn/ui on Base UI with Tailwind CSS v4). Not ticked until DS-000 (design system tooling check) runs the axe check. The owner also accepted ADR 0129 (the prototype moves into React and becomes the design source), chose `docs/design/` for the frozen copies, allowed copying markup, styles, text and fake data from the two prototype artifacts, and asked to start the port only after all 15 or 16 batches of the round 6 redesign in the POC session `parfocal-mock-round-6` have finished, leaving the design task edits to that session. DS-000 and PROTO-002 (fake data layer for the app and the catalogue) no longer wait for PROTO-001 (freeze the redesigned prototype), and PROTO-002 now also sets up React, the router, Query and the prototype index. The artifact versions at this point are kit `1793539921-4755` and mock `1793538456-0868`. Decision 15 (styling and component primitives) and the answered parts of decision 16 were removed from [decisions.md](decisions.md), and the open review question is now decision 15a.
- PROTO epic (prototype in React) loosened at the owner's request, so the whole design is clickable early. New PROTO-011 (every kit component in rough form) builds every kit component with Storybook stories, and the screen tasks depend only on it, the fake data layer and the shell screen instead of the finished DS and SHELL tasks. Rough screens need smoke tests only, and the DS tasks now depend on PROTO-011 and harden the rough components. The fake data layer no longer waits for STACK-012 (frontend testing stack). ADR 0129 (the prototype moves into React and becomes the design source) says the same, and decision 16d (when the port starts) asks whether to start now or after the redesign round.
- PROTO epic (prototype in React) added to the backlog with a research check for the fake data layer, a freeze of the redesigned prototype, the fake data layer, seven screen groups and a last task that makes the React app the design source, after the owner asked to move the UX kit and the hi-fi mock into React once the current redesign round is done. ADR 0129 (the prototype moves into React and becomes the design source) is Proposed and waits in decision 16 of [decisions.md](decisions.md). PROTO-001 (freeze the redesigned prototype) is on hold until the owner says the redesign is done. DS-000 (design system tooling check) now depends on STACK-007 (component primitives and styling) and PROTO-001, and every component task of the DS epic (design system) on DS-000, so no component starts before the styling choice and the freeze. "How to use" in the backlog says UI tasks wait for both.
- STACK-007 (component primitives and styling) now says to ask the owner first, at the owner's request. Decision 15 (styling and component primitives) in [decisions.md](decisions.md) holds the styling options (CSS Modules, Tailwind CSS v4, vanilla-extract, Panda CSS) and the primitives options (React Aria Components, Radix Primitives, Ark UI) without a default that "all recommended" would take.
- RSET-012 (large result files in bounded memory) ticked after a green `ci` run on `main` (run 41289611229). Work stopped at the owner's request. "Next up" in `docs/README.md` was rewritten into two items, one for the open decisions in [decisions.md](decisions.md) and one for the free backend tasks, because it had grown into a stale line per task.
- RSET-012 (large result files in bounded memory) built and moved to review, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. The result import step now reads the downloaded file from disk instead of memory, finds the items of the GeoJSON `features` or Parfocal JSON `objects` array with a numpy byte scanner in 1 MiB blocks (`parfocal_ingest.json_stream`) and decodes them with msgspec in batches, reads CSV in batches of 20,000 rows, builds plain polygons of a batch with shapely's vectorised calls, and keeps cells as numpy columns spilled to files in the job's work folder (`CellCollector`). A new columnar writer, `parfocal_ingest.chunk_builder`, writes the same chunk and summary bytes as `build_cells` and puts 32 chunks at a time, `pair_centres` matches into arrays, and the source file is kept with a copy inside storage. Through `python -m parfocal_ingest` against MinIO, seven imports of the 900 MB synthetic file of 1,607,244 cells peaked at 824 to 1,025 MiB of resident memory and took 42 to 45 s per step, against about 6 GiB and two minutes before, and each job was claimed once with a second worker waiting and at least 40 s of its 60 s lease left ([result](results/2026-11-01-bounded-cell-import.md)). The ceiling is set to 2 GiB as `INGEST_MEMORY_MIB`, and [ADR 0128](adr/0128-result-files-stream-through-a-byte-scanner-into-cell-columns.md) (result files stream through a byte scanner into cell columns, Proposed) records the scanner and the second writer, with decisions 14a (memory of the Modal ingest container), 14b (accept ADR 0128) and 14c (two writers of the chunk format) in [decisions.md](decisions.md). Tests: the scanner against whole-document decoding at block sizes down to one byte, the streams against the whole-file readers on the QuPath 0.7 export, the regions GeoJSON, the CSV and TSV fixtures and the Parfocal template, the streaming import against a reimplementation of the RSET-011 (cells from imported files) path down to the chunk bytes, the chunk builder against `build_cells`, a 64 MiB `tracemalloc` limit on reading 100,000 cells, and a worker process importing 120,000 generated cells under a 1 s lease without a second claim. The RSET-011 end-to-end tests pass unchanged. Surprising: `shape()` one feature at a time, not the JSON decode, was most of the old reading time, and numpy's `reduceat` with a float64 `dtype` still copies every point to float64. The benchmark file was written to the session scratchpad and deleted afterwards. No cloud service was touched.
- RSET-011 (cells from imported files) ticked after a green `ci` run on `main` (run 41283026502). ADR 0127 (imported cells keep their numbers by centre distance and take the innermost region) waits for the owner in [decisions.md](decisions.md).
- RSET-011 (cells from imported files) built and moved to review, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. The result import job now writes every cell of a GeoJSON, QuPath export, Parfocal JSON or CSV into the chunks of AIP-013 (result chunk store) with `build_cells` and `write_cells`, adds their rows with `ChunkRow.of`, and `finish` adds each class's cells from the chunk entries to the set's class counts. A cell inherits the number of an older set's cell whose centre is within 4 µm, or 16 pixels without a pixel size, which is the OBJ-002 (IDs across result sets) rule of ADR 0071 (object ID grammar), matched by the new `match_centres` on the vertex-mean centres read from each older set's summary file with a KD-tree pair search, and `match_cells` now calls it. New cells take numbers from `slide_counters` in reading order. Each cell carries the innermost region of its own set that holds its centre, smaller area first and the lower number on a tie, as [ADR 0127](adr/0127-imported-cells-keep-numbers-by-centre-and-take-the-innermost-region.md) (imported cells keep their numbers by centre distance and take the innermost region, Proposed) records, with decisions 13a (accept ADR 0127), 13b (a cell inside two regions), 13c (cells with no score) and 13d (probabilities on only some cells) in [decisions.md](decisions.md) and a note under decision 11c (cells in imported files). A file of only cells now imports, so `result-cells-only` and the `cells-not-imported` notice are gone, and a new notice `cell-probabilities-dropped` says when only some cells had probabilities. The API part of the task was already done by AIP-013 (result chunk store): `app.results` reads chunks from MinIO and nothing reads `RESULT_STORE_DIR` any more. Built as obvious behaviour: the chunks and the summary are written inside the import transaction before the set is ready, so a task made right after an import never meets `result-files-missing`, a point cell is a one-vertex outline, a QuPath nucleus outline becomes the cell's nucleus, and an older set whose summary is missing passes no numbers on instead of failing the import. The QuPath 0.7 export in the tests is the real one made by QuPath 0.7.0 for RSET-003 (readers for GeoJSON, CSV and masks), `qupath-0.7-cells.geojson` with 81 cells and one region, so no stand-in was built. Measured with the two scripts in `workers/ingest/benchmarks/` in the [cell import result](results/2026-11-01-cell-import.md): a 901 MB GeoJSON of 1,607,244 cells made from the POC's synthetic job took 110 to 120 s and up to 6.3 GB of memory through the worker's functions in three runs, of which matching a second import against the first took 1.7 to 1.8 s and inherited every number, while the reader and `build_cells` took most of the rest. That run used no database and no real worker, so RSET-012 (large result files in bounded memory) is added to read features in batches and check the lease at full size. Surprising: shapely's `centroid` is the area centroid and differs from the vertex mean the summary stores, so cells must be matched on the writer's centre or a re-import would not land on its own cells, and `KDTree.sparse_distance_matrix` keeps pairs at distance 0, which an identical re-import is made of. No migration was needed. Tests: 8 unit tests in `workers/ingest/tests/test_cell_import.py` for outlines, the innermost region, the latest set per number, one to one matching, the 4 µm edge, a full QuPath re-import and dropped probabilities, plus a large matching check, and 4 end to end tests in `apps/api/tests/test_imported_cells.py` through the API, MinIO and the real worker for the QuPath export in a ready set with per-class counts, a second import inheriting every number, a cells-only CSV, and random cells and cells below a score tasks ordering the imported cells from MinIO. No cloud service was touched.
- AIP-013 (result chunk store) ticked after a green `ci` run on `main` (run 41251883663), which frees RSET-011 (cells from imported files). ADR 0126 (cell chunks in object storage with a summary index) waits for the owner in [decisions.md](decisions.md). Work paused at the owner's request and resumes at 3 am with one task at a time, starting with anything the backlog update says must rework what was built on 2026-10-31.

## 2026-10-31

- AIP-013 (result chunk store) built and moved to review, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `parfocal_common.chunk_store.build_cells` takes a set's `Cell` values (number, outline, class, score, flags, region, and optionally type probabilities, an alternative class and a nucleus outline), bins them by the vertex mean of their float32 outline into 2048 pixel tiles as ADR 0076 (AI cells as binary chunks in object storage) says, and makes one gzip `PFC1` chunk per tile and one summary file. `write_cells` puts them under `t/{tenant}/s/{slide}/results/{set}/` as `chunk-00000.pfc` and on and `summary.pfc`, with `Content-Encoding: gzip`, eight chunks at a time and the summary last, so the retention purge of a slide deletes them with it. The summary header is the chunk index, with each chunk's tile, cell and vertex counts, number range, a count per class, unclassified and unsure, and `ChunkRow.of(entry)` turns an entry into a `result_chunks` row, so no migration was needed. The schema stays version 1: `alt_class` (the class a second typer gave, the POC's `alt_types`) is a new optional chunk and summary field, the summary gains optional `number` and `probs`, `decode` takes plain or gzip bytes and refuses a missing or unknown version with `UnknownVersionError`, and the version rule (only optional fields within a version, anything else is version 2) is written in [ADR 0126](adr/0126-cell-chunks-in-object-storage-with-a-summary-index.md) (cell chunks in object storage with a summary index, Proposed), with decisions 12a (accept ADR 0126), 12b (probabilities in the summary file) and 12c (what an alternative type is) in [decisions.md](decisions.md). `ResultStore` is now async with `get` and `put`, the API uses `ObjectResultStore` over its object store, and `LocalResultStore`, the `RESULT_STORE_DIR` setting and the `.results` folder are gone. `test_review_order.py` puts its chunks into the MinIO test bucket instead of a local folder, and the new `test_result_chunks.py` writes a set through the store, reads it back, matches the database rows with the summary index and orders a cells below a score task from MinIO. Built as obvious behaviour: a chunk that cannot be read or has an unknown version refuses a task with 409 `result-files-unreadable` instead of a 500, `ObjectStore.put_object` takes a content encoding, the writer refuses duplicate numbers, classes outside the set's list and probabilities on only some cells, and gzip runs with the time set to 0 so a rerun writes the same bytes. RSET-011 (cells from imported files) now says to add cell counts per class to the set's class counts, which `finish` counts only for regions and zones. Surprising: ADR 0076 says summary row k is C(k+1), but OBJ-002 (IDs across result sets) lets a set inherit numbers, so the summary carries `number`. httpx2 inflates a gzip-encoded object on read, so the stored bytes cannot be compared directly ([knowledge/results.md](knowledge/results.md)). No browser chunk reader exists in `packages/viewer-engine`, so nothing in TypeScript changed, and the writer was not timed. No cloud service was touched.
- RSET-004 (pairing results with slides) ticked after a green `ci` run on `main` (run 41248439457), which frees RSET-011 (cells from imported files) once AIP-013 (result chunk store) lands. ADR 0125 (result files pair in Python and import as their own job) waits for the owner in [decisions.md](decisions.md).
- RSET-004 (pairing results with slides) built and moved to review, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. The pairing rules are a pure function, `parfocal_common.pairing.pair_results`, which tries the folder shape (a result next to its slide with the same name or a name that starts with it, or in a folder named like the slide), then the file name against dropped slides and the slides already in the case, then the slide ID or file name inside the file, and answers each result with its slide, the rule and sure or likely. A tie pairs nothing and returns its candidates, and nothing matched leaves the result unmatched. `POST /api/uploads/pairings` (`pairResultFiles`) runs it for the upload wizard, and the browser sends the first 64 KiB of each result file so the slide ID inside is read on drop, before upload. `createUpload` now takes `kind: "result"` with a format and a `slide_id` or a `slide_upload_id` from the same drop, migration `0048` (result uploads) adds the columns to `uploads`, and a new `result-import` job in `parfocal_ingest.result_import` imports a paired file as a result set once its slide is ready. A result uploaded before its slide waits and is queued by the slide's publish step, an unpaired one waits until its uploader picks a slide with `PUT /api/uploads/{upload_id}/slide` (`pairUploadWithSlide`), which is refused once the import has started or ended, and `getUploadStatus` answers `waiting` with `needs-slide`, `waiting-for-slide` or `slide-refused`. A file that cannot be imported ends `failed` with a reason code such as `result-unreadable` or `result-cells-only`. [ADR 0125](adr/0125-result-files-pair-in-python-and-import-as-their-own-job.md) (result files pair in Python and import as their own job, Proposed) records the choices, and decisions 11a (accept ADR 0125), 11b (who places a waiting result), 11c (cells in imported files), 11d (what counts as likely) and 11e (masks and heatmaps in the upload) in [decisions.md](decisions.md) ask the owner. Built as obvious behaviour: a "pair by" switch for name only or ID only, as the mock's "Pair results by" has, regions and zones that overlap an older set on the slide keep their numbers (OBJ-002, IDs across result sets), the source file is kept under the slide's prefix so the purge deletes it with the slide, the whole set is written in one transaction so a killed worker leaves nothing half done, the set's id comes from the upload id so a rerun writes the same keys, and masks and heatmaps are refused at upload creation until RSET-005 (import checks and fixes). The import writes regions and zones only. Cells are counted, shown as a notice and left for the new RSET-011 (cells from imported files), which needs AIP-013 (result chunk store) and must also make the API read chunks from object storage, since `app.results` still reads local disk. Surprising: a plain `UPDATE ... WHERE slide_upload_id = ...` in the publish step would miss a result whose pairing committed after the update's snapshot, so every path locks the slide upload's row before the result's row. The INGEST-017 (publish step and slide status) test helpers moved to `apps/api/tests/upload_support.py`, and the `lab` fixture to `conftest.py`. Tests: 30 pairing unit tests, 16 API and worker tests in `test_result_uploads.py` against MinIO, and the two new routes in the permission matrix. No production or cloud service was touched. `docs/README.md` also now says SEC-009 (slide label and macro image handling) is ticked and counts 136 ticked tasks, which the SEC-009 tick on `main` had left stale.
- SEC-009 (slide label and macro image handling) ticked after a green `ci` run on `main` (run 41245281243). ADR 0124 (labels and macros only for whole-project readers in clinical projects) waits for the owner in [decisions.md](decisions.md).
- SEC-009 (slide label and macro image handling) built and moved to review, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. Guests are gone since ADR 0053 (AI results review direction), so "research access" now means a de-identified project. `GET /api/slides/{slide_id}/restricted-images/{label|macro}` (`getRestrictedImage`, `parfocal_api.restricted_images`) reads the image from storage through the API and answers it with `Cache-Control: private, no-store`, only in clinical projects and only to people who read the whole project: project owners and viewers, a workspace reviewer who is a project viewer, and a researcher with open emergency access. A task reviewer gets 403 `restricted-images-for-readers`, everyone in a de-identified project gets 404 `restricted-image-not-found`, the owner included, and a service account gets 403, because the new permission `see-labels` (researchers yes, workspace reviewers view only, project owners and viewers yes, project reviewers no) is in no scope. Every served image records `slide.restricted-image-viewed` with only `{"image": ...}`, and the audit query for a patient now includes those entries for the slides of their cases. [ADR 0124](adr/0124-labels-and-macros-only-for-whole-project-readers-in-clinical-projects.md) (labels and macros only for whole-project readers in clinical projects, Proposed) records the choices, and decisions 10a (accept ADR 0124), 10b (reviewers and labels), 10c (a switch per project), 10d (labels in a de-identified project at ingest) and 10e (the label pages inside the original file) in [decisions.md](decisions.md) ask the owner about reviewers, a per-project switch, extraction in de-identified projects and the original file. No migration was needed. Built as obvious behaviour: `getCase` lists per slide the kinds the caller may open, `exportPatientData` lists the kinds held, `erasePatient` deletes the label and macro of every slide in the patient's cases and reports `restricted_images_deleted`, because those slides stay as de-identified data, and the retention purge deletes every object under a purged slide's two prefixes in the same transaction and records `{"files": n}`, which settles the slide part of the stored-files TODO in ADR 0089 (retention purge and audit re-anchor). The retention workflow is now built by `retention_workflow(storage)`. `apiFetch` returns a `Blob` for `image/*` answers. `RESTRICTED_FILES`, `restricted_key` and `slide_prefixes` moved into `parfocal_common.ingest`, so the worker and the API name the objects the same way, and the `Store` dependency moved to `parfocal_api.context`. Surprises: the macro is as sensitive as the label, because the macros of `CMU-1.ndpi`, `Leica-1.scn`, `OS-1.bif` and `Argos-1.avs` show the label area with handwriting, numbers or a barcode, while the rendered thumbnails and overviews never show it ([knowledge/slide-formats.md](knowledge/slide-formats.md)). The original file keeps the label and macro pages on most formats, so TILES-001 (signed access to slide bytes) must keep those bytes from readers who may not see labels, which is the new threat T7 (label pages read through tile byte ranges) in the [threat model](security/threat-model.md) and decision 10e (how the original keeps its label pages out of reach). Tests: `test_restricted_images.py` (43) runs the label and the macro for owner, viewer, a workspace reviewer as viewer, emergency access, an assigned reviewer, an unassigned reviewer, a researcher with admin rights and no project role, and another workspace, each in a clinical and a de-identified project, and checks the bytes, the headers, the audit entry and that refusals record nothing, plus a service account, the case listing per role, a missing, unstored, unknown and deleted image, the patient's audit trail, and the export and erasure end to end against MinIO. The permission matrix, the isolation suite (with stand-in images that carry the tenant marker) and the project role test call the route, `test_retention.py` checks that a purged slide's files are gone and a held slide's stay, and `test_slide_ingest.py` reads the real label and macro of `CMU-1-Small-Region.svs` after ingest. On commit 1b87542 (the tick of AIP-008, job model and API, and INGEST-017, publish step and slide status) the whole `db` suite passed with 1,340 tests and 1 skip, the other suites with 533, the ingest worker's own tests with 164 and the API client tests with 10. No cloud service was touched, cost $0.
- AIP-008 (job model and API) ticked after a green `ci` run on `main` (run 41240680724), and INGEST-017 (publish step and slide status) after run 41241810851. ADR 0122 (the slide row appears only when it is ready) and ADR 0123 (model runs as engine jobs per slide) wait for the owner in [decisions.md](decisions.md).
- INGEST-017 (publish step and slide status) built and moved to review, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. The ingest workflow now runs on the uploaded file: quarantine checks the stored size and downloads the object to `{INGEST_WORK_DIR}/{job}/original{.ext}` with its SHA-256, identify names the format, scan runs the structure check of [ADR 0081](adr/0081-uploads-are-checked-by-structure.md) (uploads are checked by structure), validate reads the level table, index writes `levels.json`, derive writes the thumbnail, overview, tissue mask and label and macro images as three units, and publish moves the original out of quarantine, inserts the slide in its project and case with its facts, records `slide.published` and sends the uploader an `upload-done` notification in one transaction ([knowledge/jobs.md](knowledge/jobs.md)). Identify now runs before scan, because the structure check needs the format, so `STEP_NAMES` changed order. The worker downloads the whole file rather than reading ranges, because OpenSlide needs a local path, an MRXS slide needs its unpacked folder and ADR 0116 (resumable uploads with S3 multipart and Uppy) takes the SHA-256 at that copy, as [ADR 0043](adr/0043-server-slide-reading.md) (server slide reading) rule 8 says. A worker that resumes a job elsewhere downloads it again. [ADR 0122](adr/0122-ingest-publishes-the-slide-row-only-when-it-is-ready.md) (the slide row appears only when it is ready, Proposed) records the model: no slide row exists until publish, so no slide list changed, and the upload carries the status. `GET /api/uploads/{upload_id}/status` (`getUploadStatus`) is the live status until STACK-024 (realtime transport) is decided, for the upload's creator, with the values `uploading`, `processing` (with the next step), `ready` (with the slide ID and notices) and `failed` (with a reason code and plain message, `upload-aborted`, `upload-expired`, `upload-missing`, `size-mismatch` or a refusal such as `unknown` or `truncated`). The refusal and notice texts moved to `parfocal_common.ingest` so the API never imports the worker. Published keys hold no file name: `t/{tenant}/s/{slide}/original{.ext}` with the level table and previews next to it, and labels and macros under `t/{tenant}/restricted/{slide}/`, which no route serves until SEC-009 (slide label and macro image handling). The original leaves quarantine by `UploadPartCopy` in 1 GiB parts, because R2 caps a single copy below 5 GiB, and the quarantine object is deleted after publishing and after a refusal. Migration `0047` (slide ingest), with `down_revision` `0046` (model runs from AIP-008, job model and API, which merges first), adds `upload_id` and the file facts to `slides`, a key on `uploads (id, project_id)` for the composite foreign key, and label indexes that ignore deleted slides as ADR 0019 (soft delete policy) asks. `parfocal_common.object_store` gained put, head, delete, get, a streamed download with SHA-256, a multipart copy and a prefix listing. The storage settings moved to `StorageSettings` in `parfocal_common.settings`, shared by the API and the new `IngestSettings`, and `parfocal-ingest` now depends on `parfocal-api` for the slide tables, the audit log and notifications. Paired result files were left to RSET-004 (pairing results with slides), because the upload metadata has no way to mark a result file, and its backlog entry now says so. No model runs: the end to end test checks that the workspace holds only the ingest job and no result set. Built as obvious behaviour: blocking reader calls run in a thread so the lease heartbeat keeps running, reader errors during a step become `unreadable` instead of a crash loop, a job input without an upload ID fails as `upload-missing`, a second file with a taken name gets the label "Name (2)", and a rerun of publish adds no second slide or notification. A failed upload records `upload.refused` and sends no notification, because the catalogue line of `upload-done` says the upload is ready. TODO for the owner in ADR 0122: whether a failure gets its own kind and wording, with INGEST-018 (ingest failure UX). Tests: `test_slide_ingest.py` (8 tests) uploads the real `CMU-1-Small-Region.svs` into a case through the API with presigned part `PUT`s to MinIO, runs the job engine's worker in a thread while it polls the status route through uploading, processing and ready, and checks the slide facts, the case view, every stored key, the restricted prefix, the deleted quarantine object, the notification and the audit entry. It refuses an EICAR string as `unknown` and a cut-off `synthetic-jpeg.tiff` as `truncated` with no slide, no stored files and no notification, kills a real worker process with SIGKILL after the first derive unit and has a second worker with an empty work folder finish it with each unit written once, one slide and one notification, numbers a taken label, reads a cancelled upload and a stopped job as failed, and keeps the status to its uploader. `test_object_storage.py` gained the multipart copy in three parts with listing and deletes, and the streamed download. `test_slide_labels.py` (9) covers labels, extensions and failure messages. The permission matrix and the isolation suite list `getUploadStatus`. The `db` CI job now fetches and caches the `small` fixture group with `PARFOCAL_REQUIRE_FIXTURES=small`. Rebased onto commit 441de82 (the committed decisions file, on top of AIP-008), the whole `db` suite passed with 1,284 tests and 1 skip, the other suites with 533, and the ingest worker's own tests with 164. The old skeleton test that ran placeholder steps was removed, and the job isolation test now queues the test workflow, because a queued ingest job with no upload would be claimed by the real steps. Surprise: the job engine claims any queued ingest job in the database, and the upload tests leave completed uploads with queued jobs behind, so `work()` passes a workspace ID through to the `claim()` of AIP-008 (job model and API) and `python -m parfocal_ingest` gained `--workspace`, and the new tests run workers for their own workspace only. Rebased onto AIP-008 (job model and API), an ingest job that is cancelled or cancelling reads as `failed` with `check-stopped`. No cloud service was touched, cost $0.
- The owner's decisions file moved from the git-ignored `tmp/decisions.md` to [decisions.md](decisions.md), because agents work in their own worktrees and an ignored file there was deleted with the worktree. `AGENTS.md` now says agents add their questions to it in the same change as their task.
- AIP-008 (job model and API) built and moved to review, not ticked: it waits for a green CI run on `main`. Model runs are jobs of the kind `model-run` in the shared engine of `parfocal_common.jobs`, one per slide with the six stages JOBS-004 (job window with steps per slide) draws, and a `model_runs` row keyed by the job id holds the slide, an optional review task, a `batch_id` shared by the slides started together, the scope (view, selection or whole slide), the detector and typer as name and version strings until AIP-002 (model registry) exists, the test-time views (1 or 8) and the tile order (`raster` or `tumor-first`) ([ADR 0123](adr/0123-model-runs-are-engine-jobs-per-slide.md), model runs as engine jobs per slide, Proposed). The engine gained `paused`, `cancelling` and `cancelled`, `jobs.pause_reason`, `job_steps.units_total`, `StepPausedError`, `JobStoppedError`, `StepRun.set_total` and `should_stop`, a `move` function for cancel, resume and retry, and a `finish` that only the worker holding the job can call. Resume and retry keep done stages and recorded tiles. A claim ends cancelling jobs whose lease ran out and takes an optional workspace. `parfocal_api.model_runs` serves `startModelRuns`, `listModelRuns`, `getModelRun`, `cancelModelRun`, `resumeModelRun` and `retryModelRun` under `/api/projects/{project}/model-runs`, behind the new permission `run-models` (researchers, and in a project only owners) for writes and `see-slides` for reads, so reviewers see only the runs on their task slides and none while their first read is open. Start, cancel, resume and retry are audited as `model-run.*`. Migration `0046` (model runs) sits on `0045` (uploads). It merged before INGEST-017 (publish step and slide status), which takes `0047`. `test_model_runs.py` drives the stages with fake step functions through `claim_model_run` and `run_job`: a failed run keeps two of four tiles and a retry does only the other two, a pause resumes at its tile, a cancel in the middle of a stage keeps its finished tiles and its Undo finishes the run, a dead worker's cancel is settled by the next claim, and a worker that lost its lease cannot end the run. The routes are in the permission matrix, the isolation suite and the project role test. Surprises: pydantic's lax `Literal[1, 8]` takes `True` as 1 and `8.0` as 8, so a `BeforeValidator` refuses non-integers, and a claim without a workspace in a test can take another test's queued job, because every test in a session shares one database. JOBS-000 (research check for the model run contract) stays open, since this fixes how a run is stored and controlled, not how a model is called. No GPU work, no Modal call and no cloud cost.
- Agent guide: the owner asked for one file of open decisions. `AGENTS.md` now has "The owner's decisions file": every open question goes into the git-ignored `tmp/decisions.md` with lettered options, the recommended one in bold and a blank `Your choice:` line, and answered items are applied, logged and deleted.
- TASK-004 (blinding and first read), NOTIF-007 (inbox API, with the mute rework) and EVAL-007 (study template model and API) ticked after a green `ci` run on `main` (run 41223419727). INGEST-001 (upload API with presigned multipart) stays in review until INGEST-028 (upload protocol check on the R2 dev bucket) has run. ADR 0117 (review order stored per task slide), ADR 0118 (blinding and a kept first read), ADR 0119 (inbox mutes), ADR 0120 (study templates as workspace rows that a task only pins) and ADR 0121 (S3 client signed by hand on httpx2) wait for the owner. "Next up" item 1 in `docs/README.md` listed NOTIF-004 (email templates without PHI), NOTIF-005 (digest and quiet hours) and MLEVAL-002 (geometric scorer) as free although all three were ticked, so it now lists the backend tasks that `board.py ready` shows.
- INGEST-001 (upload API with presigned multipart) built and moved to review, not ticked: its Done when line also needs INGEST-028 (upload protocol check on the R2 dev bucket), which needs the owner's yes for a `tofu apply`, and then a green CI run on `main`. `parfocal_api.uploads` implements Uppy's Companion S3 routes under `/api/uploads` as [ADR 0116](adr/0116-resumable-uploads-with-s3-multipart-and-uppy.md) (resumable uploads with S3 multipart and Uppy) decided: `createUpload`, `signUploadPart`, `listUploadParts`, `completeUpload` and `abortUpload`, plus `findOpenUpload` for a reloaded page. Migration `0045` (uploads) adds the `uploads` table with row-level security, the key `t/{tenant}/quarantine/{upload}/{safe name}`, the part size and count, the creator, the storage upload ID and `last_activity_at`. The Python S3 client is signed by hand on httpx2 in `parfocal_common.object_store` ([ADR 0121](adr/0121-s3-client-signed-by-hand-on-httpx2.md), S3 client signed by hand, Proposed), because boto3 is synchronous and aiobotocore brings aiohttp and a pinned botocore for five operations. Its signatures match two AWS documented examples. Tests run against MinIO, now the `storage` service in `compose.yaml` with the spike's pinned image, which the `db` CI job already starts, and `PARFOCAL_TEST_STORAGE_URL` points a session at another MinIO. The largest upload is 20 GiB (`UPLOAD_MAX_BYTES`), about three times the 7 GB fixture and 1,280 parts at 16 MiB, and a larger create is 400 `upload-too-large`. Uploading takes `upload-slides` as a project owner, which researchers hold and reviewers do not, admin rights add nothing, and an upload may name a case of its project. Only its creator reaches an upload afterwards. A completion lists every page of parts and is refused with 409 `upload-parts-invalid` unless they run from 1 without gaps, every part but the last has the stored size and the ETags match, and then it queues the ingest job with only the upload ID as input, since the file name is PHI. The ingest kind and step names moved to `parfocal_common.ingest` so the API can queue the job without importing the worker. `parfocal_api.stale_uploads` aborts uploads with no signature or listing for 24 hours, which bounds the last part `PUT` because a part URL lives 15 minutes. Its hourly trigger waits for the cloud cron like the retention purge. `infra/tofu/cloudflare.tf` gains the bucket CORS rule and a lifecycle rule that aborts incomplete multipart uploads after 1 day, on the prefix `t/` because a prefix cannot skip the tenant in `t/{tenant}/quarantine/`. `tofu fmt` and `tofu validate` pass, nothing was planned or applied. Built as obvious behaviour: a second completion answers the first result, a second abort answers `aborted`, abort answers 200 with JSON because Uppy parses every answer, a storage refusal at completion (`InvalidPart`, `EntityTooSmall`) becomes the same 409 and rolls back the queued job, an upload storage no longer knows is 409 `upload-gone`, a demoted owner or an archived project stops an upload mid-way, and safe names keep their extension. Tests: `test_uploads.py` (14 tests) covers the lifecycle to a queued ingest job with real part `PUT`s, ListParts beyond one page, size limits, uneven parts, gaps and wrong ETags, the open upload lookup, abort, the stale upload job, who reaches an upload, cases, archived projects, a storage refusal, and another workspace failing to sign, list, complete or abort. `test_object_storage.py` (10 tests) checks safe names, the shared ingest plan, ListParts paging against MinIO, a tampered part URL and wrong credentials, and `parfocal_common` gained `test_part_plan.py` (18) and `test_object_store.py` (6). The permission matrix seeds three real uploads per workspace for its six new calls, and the isolation suite seeds an upload whose part ETag is a marker. Dropping the part size check made the uneven part test fail. The migration was tested on top of migration `0042` (first reads), the head of `origin/main` at the time, and is committed with `down_revision` `0044` (study templates), because migration `0043` (muted notifications) and EVAL-007 (study template model and API), which writes `0044`, merge first. Surprises: MinIO answers a completion of uneven parts with `EntityTooSmall` only when a part is under 5 MiB, so without our check a local test would have passed. A stack started before this change needs `pnpm db:up` once more to add MinIO. No cloud service was touched, cost $0.
- EVAL-007 (study template model and API) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. The report block catalogue is `parfocal_common.report_blocks`, a closed list of eleven block kinds from ADR 0097 (report templates built from blocks): `headline-numbers`, `region-metrics`, `confusion-matrix`, `reviewer-agreement`, `disagreement-reasons`, `opinion-answers`, `worst-objects`, `slide-table`, `slide-figure`, `text` and `comparison`. Each has the shared title, show title, width and page break and its own settings, taken from the round 6 mock's block editor, and `ReportFormat` holds the page and format settings. Unknown kinds, unknown settings and mistyped settings are refused with the 422 `validation` problem. `parfocal_api.study_templates` adds the presets (`objects_to_check`, `opinion_form`, `blinding`, `first_read`, `start_mode`, `split`), and the routes `listStudyTemplates`, `getStudyTemplate`, `listStudyTemplateVersions`, `getStudyTemplateVersion`, `createStudyTemplate`, `editStudyTemplate`, `retireStudyTemplate` and `restoreStudyTemplate` under `/api/study-templates`, guarded by `report-templates`. Migration `0044` (study templates), whose down revision is `0043` (muted notifications from NOTIF-007, inbox API), adds `study_templates` and `study_template_versions` and the pin columns `study_template_id` and `study_template_version` on `review_tasks`. The five built-ins (sentinel node AI evaluation, detection, segmentation, cell typing and the reader study) are rows in every workspace, seeded by `create_tenant` and, for existing workspaces, by the migration. An edit that changes anything makes a new version, an identical one returns the current version, and a task pins the latest version or the one it names and is refused for an unknown or retired template ([ADR 0120](adr/0120-study-templates-are-workspace-rows-and-tasks-only-pin-them.md), study templates as workspace rows that a task only pins, Proposed). Presets are template data only and the API does not fill task fields from them, which TASK-009 (study template in a new review task) now does in the stepper. Built as obvious behaviour: restore after retire, for the library's Undo, and a task count per template, which ADMIN-008 (report template library) shows. Tests: catalogue unit tests, API tests for the seeded five through the platform route, create with every block kind, a name taken, edit as a new version with the old one intact, a built-in edited, retire and restore with audit entries, eleven refusals, isolation, the task pin and its audit details, the refusals for a task, and the migration seeding an existing workspace, plus permission matrix entries for every new route and isolation seeders for both tables. Open for the owner: a task has one scope kind, so the sentinel node template presets all regions only, the mock's reader study and outline quality forms are not defined, and the cell typing sample of 200 cells is a placeholder. Surprises: two `Title` type aliases in different modules split the OpenAPI schema into `Title-Input` and `Title-Output`, and a platform insert into a tenant table needs its own policy and no `RETURNING` ([knowledge/api.md](knowledge/api.md), [knowledge/database.md](knowledge/database.md)).
- NOTIF-007 (inbox API) changed at the owner's request, still not ticked until CI is green on `main`. Muting must not throw notifications away, so [ADR 0119](adr/0119-inbox-mutes-for-projects-tasks-and-cases.md) (inbox mutes, still Proposed) was rewritten in place: it now weighs dropping muted events, keeping and silencing them, and keeping and silencing them with work kinds breaking through, and picks the last. Migration `0043` (muted notifications) adds `notifications.muted`. `notify()` now stores a muted event with the flag set, `realtime` off and no email, so it never pushes, emails or joins a digest. Unmuting sends nothing old, and muted rows stay in the inbox. Each catalogue entry must declare `breaks_mute`, a keyword field without a default, and `BREAKS_MUTES` holds mentions, task assigned, task due soon and emergency access, because those hand the person work or are addressed to them. The catalogue has no reassign or nudge kind. Disagreements ready stays mutable because it reports that reviewers finished, which is a judgement call the owner may want to revisit. `listNotifications` gained `muted` (false by default, so the bell and the main list leave muted rows out), items carry `muted`, and `getNotificationCounts` counts only unmuted rows per kind and reports `muted` and `muted_unread` apart. Mark all read includes muted rows. Tests: `test_inbox.py` now checks that a muted event is stored with the flag and no push or email, that an assignment, a due date, a mention and emergency access in a muted task or project arrive as normal notifications, that unmuting leaves the old muted row in place and sends nothing, the `muted` filter, counts without muted rows, marking muted rows read and the counts across workspaces. A catalogue test fails when the `breaks_mute` field gets a default or the set changes, and a migration test checks that existing rows read as not muted. A mutation check that let muted rows email or took task due soon out of the set failed the tests. The OpenAPI client and the data map were regenerated, and `docs/knowledge/api.md` and `docs/knowledge/database.md` were updated. Tested on top of `0041` (inbox mutes), the head of `origin/main` at the time, and it was renumbered to `0043` on top of migration `0042` (first reads) from TASK-004 (blinding and first read) when it merged. No cloud service was touched, cost $0.
- TASK-004 (blinding and first read) backend built, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0118](adr/0118-blinding-and-a-kept-first-read.md) (blinding and a kept first read, Proposed) keeps the switches on the task as ADR 0078 (review task model) put them, with project defaults left to PROJ-008 (project settings) and template presets to EVAL-007 (study template model and API). A project reviewer now reads another reviewer's decisions, layer, opinion, layer export, first read and added objects on a task slide once they have submitted it, or at once in a task that is not blinded, while project owners and viewers read everything and admin rights add nothing. In a task with `ai_hidden_first_read`, `POST .../first-read` (`submitFirstRead`) copies the opinion answers and the reviewer's added regions and cells into `review_assignments.first_read`, and migration `0042` (first reads) adds the columns and a trigger that refuses any later change, even for the table owner. Until then a project reviewer gets no AI output on that slide: the result set routes leave the set out or answer `ai-hidden`, the layer export answers `ai-hidden` and the layer summary drops its counts. Decisions and edits answer `first-read-open` and submit answers `first-read-needed` for every assignee. The rules live in `parfocal_api.blinding`, and `test_blinding.py` checks the owner, a researcher with admin rights and no project role, a viewer, the assigned reviewer and an unassigned reviewer in each phase (before the first read, before submit, after submit) of four task kinds, plus the kept first read and the refusals. Two mutations of the rules each failed tests. Built as obvious behaviour: an added object read by its id (`getAnnotation`, `listAnnotationHistory`) is blinded like the list, the decision and edit writes stop answering `unknown-object` while the AI is hidden, since that told a reviewer how many regions exist, hiding works per slide so a second task on the same slide cannot leak it, and `ai_hidden_first_read` can no longer be switched once a reviewer has started (409 `task-started`). TASK-004 was split: its E2E moved to the new TASK-010 (reveal the AI after a first read, end to end), which depends on TASK-004, GUIDE-002 (guided review) and GUIDE-007 (opinion form for the reviewer), and TASK-004's Done when line now names the API tests. TASK-007 (nudge, reassign and change a task), TASK-008 (submit a slide review) and COLLAB-002 (threads API) gained a line each. Migration `0042` names migration `0041` (inbox mutes) from NOTIF-007 (inbox API) as its parent, which merges first on top of migration `0040` (review order) from GUIDE-012 (least sure first order). It was tested on top of migration `0039` (admin rights), because neither was in this branch. Surprise: POC doc 44 (round 6 working mock), written today, reads the first read as hiding only the AI's claim and picking each object's class blind. That needs a decision style model no backlog task holds, so the stricter reading was built and the ADR asks the owner to choose.
- STACK-039 (resumable upload protocol) and GUIDE-012 (least sure first order, regions and cells) ticked after a green `ci` run on `main` (run 41219425324). [ADR 0117](adr/0117-review-order-is-stored-per-task-slide.md) (review order stored per task slide) is still Proposed and waits for the owner.
- NOTIF-007 (inbox API) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. Since ADR 0053 (AI results review direction) cases are optional, so a mute of cases alone would catch almost nothing in the catalogue. [ADR 0119](adr/0119-inbox-mutes-for-projects-tasks-and-cases.md) (inbox mutes, Proposed) therefore lets a person mute a project, a review task or a case. Slides are left out, muted events are not stored at all, and mentions and emergency access still get through, because a mention is written to one person on purpose. Migration `0041` (inbox mutes) adds `project_id`, `task_id` and `case_id` to `notifications`, fills them for existing rows one workspace at a time, and adds `notification_mutes` with row-level security. `notify()` reads the three ids from the target and skips muted recipients, and a new mute marks that person's pending emails for the subject as skipped, so digests and quiet-hours holds do not send them later. New routes in `parfocal_api.inbox`: `listNotifications` (kinds, read state, keyset pages like My work), `getNotificationCounts` (total and unread per kind, for the bell's tabs), `markNotificationsRead` (ids, or all with kinds and an `up_to` time), and `listNotificationMutes`, `muteNotifications` and `unmuteNotifications`. Targets are still resolved on open as ADR 0091 (notification catalogue and targets) decided. Built as obvious behaviour: `up_to` on mark all read, so an item that arrives after the panel was drawn stays unread, and a partial index on unread rows. Tests: `test_inbox.py` (10 tests) covers kind and read filters, pages without gaps, counts, bulk read that leaves another person's ids and earlier read times alone, mark all read with kinds and `up_to`, muting a task, a case and a project with mentions and emergency access getting through, pending emails skipped by a mute and never sent, who may mute what, and the inbox, counts and mutes staying in their workspace. A migration test fills the context of existing rows, and the permission and isolation suites list every new route and the new table. A mutation check that dropped the mute check in `notify()`, the owner filter in the inbox or the mention exception when skipping emails failed the tests. The OpenAPI client and the data map were regenerated, and the permission page did not change because every new route is open to members. Surprises: no single mute call works for every seat in the permission matrix, because the researcher with admin rights holds no role in the shared project, so the mute route is listed as `own-notification`. The `tenant_isolation` policy ignores the platform scope, so the backfill sets `app.tenant_id` per workspace ([knowledge/database.md](knowledge/database.md), [knowledge/api.md](knowledge/api.md)). The migration was tested on top of `0039` and points at `0041` (blinding and first read) for the merge. No cloud service was touched, cost $0.
- GUIDE-012 (least sure first order) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. The owner asked for cells to be ordered too, so the first version, which ordered only AI regions, was reworked before anything was pushed. `createReviewTask` now applies the task's scope with `in_scope`, rates every object in scope, orders them least sure first and stores the result as one packed array per task slide in `review_order` (migration `0040`, review order), five bytes per object. `GET /api/review-tasks/{task}/slides/{slide}/order` returns the total and a page ([ADR 0117](adr/0117-review-order-is-stored-per-task-slide.md), review order stored per task slide, Proposed). A region's certainty is its class probability, else its score. A cell's certainty is its chunk's `probs` byte for its AI class, and an imported chunk without `probs` gives unscored cells, which follow in the task's seeded random order from TASK-003 (scope and progress). The cells below a score scope filters on the detection score. Cells are read from their `PFC1` chunk files through a new `ResultStore` protocol (`get(object_key) -> bytes`) with a local directory store, because nothing wrote or read chunk bytes before. Buckets, a CDN and signing stay with STACK-025 (object storage, CDN and signed access). Containment for the inside scopes runs in PostGIS on each chunk's cell centres. A scope change rebuilds the order with the stored seed, and a chunk file that is missing refuses the task with 409 `result-files-missing`. Computing the order inside the request is accepted for now, and the new GUIDE-013 (review order as a background job) moves it onto the job engine. Unit tests cover known scores, ties, a set without scores, a mixed set, cell certainty with and without `probs`, the pooled random sample against `in_scope`, the packing and the local store. API tests cover every scope kind on a slide with two chunk files, two reviewers and the owner getting the same order, pages, a scope change, a missing file, and a recalibration simulated by rewriting stored region values and a chunk's `probs` ([knowledge/database.md](knowledge/database.md)). On a synthetic 1.6 million cell slide, ordering a random 200 cells took 0.36 to 0.80 s and a cells below a score scope of 162,500 cells took 1.3 to 2.7 s on an M4 laptop, without file or database time ([result](results/2026-10-31-review-order-large-slide.md)). Existing tasks get no order rows, since no shared database has running tasks.
- STACK-039 (resumable upload protocol) follow-up: the `browser` CI job failed on `main` (run 41217209113) because the latency proxy's stats gained `uploadedBytes` and `perf/request-latency.spec.ts` compared the whole object. The test now expects `uploadedBytes: 0`. The spike's checks ran `pnpm test` but not `pnpm test:browser`, so a change to `packages/latency-proxy` needs both.
- The owner accepted [ADR 0116](adr/0116-resumable-uploads-with-s3-multipart-and-uppy.md) (resumable uploads with S3 multipart and Uppy). IAM-024 (admin as a right of a researcher) ticked after a green `ci` run on `main` (run 41216737418), which also frees EVAL-007 (study template model and API).
- STACK-039 (resumable upload protocol) spiked and proposed, not ticked. [ADR 0116](adr/0116-resumable-uploads-with-s3-multipart-and-uppy.md) (resumable uploads with S3 multipart and Uppy, Proposed) keeps the default of ADR 0003 (pilot platform architecture): Uppy 6 in Companion mode in the browser sends only part `PUT` requests to R2, and the API creates, signs one 16 MiB part at a time, lists parts with pagination, checks that every part but the last has one size and completes. Tus and Google-style uploads were set aside because both need a server in the byte path. `packages/upload-spike` runs the published Uppy bundle in headless Chromium against MinIO in Docker through the latency proxy, cuts the link at 30% and closes the page at 60%. A 4 GiB synthetic file over 200 Mbit/s and 50 ms RTT took 224.97 s, resumed both times, signed no stored part again, resent 15,351,808 and 14,123,008 bytes (the part in flight) and produced an object with the source SHA-256. The real 1.99 GB `OS-1.ndpi` did the same, because no 4 GB NDPI fixture exists ([result](results/2026-10-31-resumable-upload-spike.md)). Built as obvious behaviour: the latency proxy gained an uplink limit and an uploaded byte count, since it only throttled downloads. Surprises: R2 cannot presign POSTs, so Uppy's `signRequest` mode does not fit it. MinIO accepted uneven parts that R2 refuses and sent CORS headers on an expired URL where R2 sends none. MinIO refused an `AbortIncompleteMultipartUpload` lifecycle rule. The official MinIO images no longer pull, so the spike pins the community build `pgsty/minio`. Uppy sends one part per file at a time and every part costs a CORS preflight, which used 77 to 80% of the link at 50 ms RTT. INGEST-001 (upload API) and INGEST-002 (upload UI with resume) were edited to follow the ADR, INGEST-028 (upload protocol check on the R2 dev bucket) was added, and the lessons are in [knowledge/uploads.md](knowledge/uploads.md). No cloud service was touched, cost $0. The ADR waits for the owner.
- IAM-024 (admin as a right of a researcher) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `Role` is now only `researcher` or `reviewer`, and migration `0039` (admin rights) adds `admin_rights` to `memberships` and `invitations`, turns every `admin` row into a researcher with admin rights, and adds a check that only a researcher holds the flag ([ADR 0096](adr/0096-researcher-and-reviewer-with-admin-rights.md), researcher and reviewer with admin rights). The matrix in `parfocal_api.permissions` has two columns and an `ADMIN_RIGHTS` set that adds `manage-people`, `workspace-settings`, `integrations`, `report-templates` and `audit-log` and nothing else. `report-templates` is new, with view only for researchers, so EVAL-007 (study template model and API) can guard its routes with it. `PUT /api/tenant/members/{user_id}/admin-rights` gives or removes the right, refuses a reviewer (409 `admin-rights-need-researcher`) and the last holder (409 `last-admin`), and records `member.admin-rights-given` or `member.admin-rights-removed`. A holder may give up their own right while another holder remains, and a holder's role cannot change to reviewer (409 `holds-admin-rights`). Invitations, `addMember`, the member list and `/api/me` workspaces carry `admin_rights`, and accepted invitations and `member.added` record it. The permission page lists each role's responsibilities and an "Added by admin rights" column, and the OpenAPI client and the data map were regenerated. Tests: the matrix test runs every guarded and member endpoint for a researcher, a researcher with admin rights and a reviewer, a migration test turns an admin membership and invitation into a researcher with admin rights and back, `test_admin_rights.py` checks the audit entries, the last holder, the reviewer refusal and a signed-in session that gains and loses the audit log with the right, and tests that used the admin role now use a researcher with admin rights, which may now create projects and cases. Surprises: two seats that may both delete, sign off or erase broke the shared matrix workspace, so each seat now gets its own, and the local migration owner is a superuser that bypasses row-level security, so the migration sets the platform scope before it rewrites rows ([knowledge/database.md](knowledge/database.md)).
- Thesis material moved out of this public repository. The owner's thesis plan, its guidance and its tasks now live in the private repository `fedasevich/parfocal-thesis` next to this one, because FIT's plagiarism check compares a thesis with what is published online. `docs/project/thesis.md` was removed and its index rows dropped. The final assignment promises a review that starts with the outputs the model is least sure of and measured performance of the whole application, and no task built either, so [ADR 0114](adr/0114-thesis-assignment-tasks.md) (tasks the thesis assignment adds) added GUIDE-012 (least sure first order) and TEST-019 (application performance on main paths), and GUIDE-002 (guided review) now depends on GUIDE-012. A parallel session committed part of this change inside `b89c6da` (billing caps and alerts) because both sessions share one working tree, so that commit already holds the backlog, agent guide and ADR index edits.
- The owner accepted [ADR 0115](adr/0115-production-access-and-spend-caps.md) (production access and spend caps). Neon and Cloudflare CLI sign-ins are done. Neon project `neon-red-engine`, created on 2026-10-31 at 11:20 UTC by someone other than this session, was left untouched for the owner to identify.
- OPS-009 (storage lifecycle and cost controls) partly done, not ticked. The owner asked for access to the real infrastructure with budgets and alerts everywhere. Every billing account was checked on 2026-10-31 and all spend this cycle is $0.00. Modal has a hard cap ($30 of free credits, spend limit $0). Neon (Free, no card), Vercel (Hobby, no card), Zitadel (Free) and Cloudflare Workers (Free) are capped by plan. GitHub already had $0 budgets that block Actions, Packages, Codespaces and Git LFS. Cloudflare R2 is pay as you go with a card and no hard cap, so its account budget alert was lowered from $10 to $1. The table is in [knowledge/platform.md](knowledge/platform.md) and the access paths in [knowledge/agent-tooling.md](knowledge/agent-tooling.md). [ADR 0115](adr/0115-production-access-and-spend-caps.md) (production access and spend caps, Proposed) makes production read-only for agents unless the owner approves each write, and `AGENTS.md` has a new section, "Real infrastructure and production". Neon and Cloudflare still need the owner's one-time CLI sign-in.
- Owner decisions recorded. The owner accepted ADRs 0052 (the task board), 0098 (Viv and deck.gl versions), 0099 (TIFF and Zarr readers in the browser), 0100 (viewer state outside React), 0101 (golden image comparison), 0102 (the virtual RGB ladder for Viv), 0103 (memory settings and the memory test baseline), 0104 (camera motion), 0105 (decode workers behind one byte cache), 0106 (camera bounds and feel profiles), 0107 (viewer input binding), 0108 (overlay layers), 0109 (idle viewer and hidden tabs), 0110 (native JPEG decode and the tissue fit), 0111 (JPEG 2000 in the decode workers), 0112 (finest level sampling and WebGL recovery) and 0113 (interaction signal, units and touch taps) as written. The owner kept every recommended default for VIEW-026 (overlay layer system: styles, stacking order, keeping hidden layers in memory, per-part opacity), VIEW-033 (hidden tab keeps loading), VIEW-032 (count only the page's own idle time), VIEW-020 (cubic ease in and out), VIEW-007 (nearest when magnifying), VIEW-034 (the graphics failure message and limits), VIEW-023 (double tap goes to 40x), VIEW-024 (the 1.25 factor for the scan objective), VIEW-035 (tick with the stand-in test) and VIEW-011 (grey damaged tiles). The UI still waits. Follow-ups went into the backlog: VIEW-027 (outline casings) compares the overlay styles with the POC and with pathology display conventions, VIEW-007 is compared with the POC, VIEW-011 records the owner's grey checkerboard idea, CASES-009 (search API) measures its 100 ms budget on one fixed reference machine, and VIEW-054 (JPEG 2000 slide in the viewer test set) adds both slides. The owner said small timing differences on shared machines are not worth chasing.
- Cloud open again. The owner opened Modal, Neon and the Zitadel CI credentials, which unblocks STACK-019 (web framework), STACK-020 (database hosting) and IAM-001 (login provider setup), and asked that it cost as little as possible. The cost rules are in [knowledge/platform.md](knowledge/platform.md) and a working rule in `AGENTS.md`.
