## 2026-10-26

- VIEW-023 (touch navigation), VIEW-024 (coordinate systems and units) and VIEW-035 (model inference pause during camera motion) ticked after a green `ci` run on `main` (run 40295679155). VIEW-035 was measured against a stand-in busy job and is repeated with the real region pass once a browser inference task exists.
- VIEW-011 (SVS JPEG 2000 through OpenJPEG WASM) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. Aperio JPEG 2000 tiles (compression 33003 and 33005) now decode through `@cornerstonejs/codec-openjpeg` 1.3.6 inside the decode workers, in `packages/viewer-engine/src/pixel-source/tiff/jpeg2000`. geotiff imports the decoder on the first tile and the decoder imports the codec on its first decode, so JPEG and deflate slides never request the 260,334 byte WebAssembly file (browser test with a J2K slide as positive control). A tile with no codestream marker, a decode error or a wrong size draws as flat grey 160, a page refuses to decode with `MainThreadCodecError`, and the OpenJPEG output is already RGB so no second colour transform runs (mean absolute error 0.40 to 0.48 against OpenSlide at level 0 of JP2K-33003-1.svs and 0.00 to 0.05 on CMU-1-JP2K-33005.svs). Added Node and browser tests, synthetic J2K fixtures, three J2K goldens, [ADR 0111](adr/0111-jpeg-2000-in-the-decode-workers-and-ome-channel-pages.md) (Proposed) and [a result file](results/2026-10-26-jpeg-2000-in-the-decode-workers.md). Surprises: the harness build needed `worker.format es` or the codec would be inlined in every decode worker, the earlier registration of compression 34712 was removed because its payload may be a JP2 file, and `slide-formats.md` now says OpenJPEG returns RGB for both Aperio variants while ingest's decoder returns YCbCr. One tile each at levels 1 and 2 of the two 33003 slides differs from OpenSlide by up to 47, which is unexplained and is the new task VIEW-055 (JPEG 2000 coarse level differences from OpenSlide). The new task VIEW-054 (JPEG 2000 slide in the viewer fixture group) puts the real J2K goldens in browser CI.
- VIEW-010 (generic tiled TIFF and SVS JPEG) closed its only gap, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. CMU-1.svs and CMU-1.tiff already passed the PixelSource conformance suite and the CMU-1.svs goldens existed, so generic TIFF got goldens of its own (`viv-cmu-1-tiff-10x`, `viv-synthetic-jpeg-tiff-fit`, `viv-synthetic-sparse-tiff-fit`). The sparse golden shows white glass for absent tiles, in contrast to grey for damaged JPEG 2000 tiles. All of them matched after the finest-level sampling of VIEW-007 (interpolation rule) landed.
- VIEW-012 (OME-TIFF 8-bit and 16-bit) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. A multichannel OME-TIFF stores each channel as its own page with its own SubIFDs, which the source read as one grey channel. `ome-channels.ts` now reads the channel list from the OME XML and the source returns one plane per channel page, with names and colours. `src/viv/viv-channel-levels.ts` turns such a source into Viv c, y, x levels and layer props, and a Deck with Viv's `MultiscaleImageLayer` draws the 8-bit and 16-bit synthetic fixtures through per-channel contrast limits (blue 74.8 at default limits, 149.6 with the upper limit halved, in both bit depths) ([result](results/2026-10-26-ome-channel-contrast-through-viv.md)). `createVivRoute` still draws RGB and the decode workers still read RGB regions, so mounting channel levels in the route, worker channel reads and the one-frame contrast timing moved to VIEW-018 (multichannel fluorescence display), and VIEW-012's Done-when line says so. The channel reader only handles Z and T stacks when the dimension order starts with `XYC`.
- VIEW-007 (interpolation rule) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. Viv draws interleaved RGB tiles through its own `BitmapLayer`, which always builds a linear texture and ignores the `interpolation` prop, so the route had no finest level rule. `packages/viewer-engine/src/viv/interpolation.ts` now patches `BitmapLayer.prototype.updateState` once and gives tiles with `tileId.z === 0` a shared sampler with nearest magnification and linear minification, one per device. Coarser tiles and the background image are untouched. The half-texel inset is met by one texture per tile with `clamp-to-edge`, which keeps a bilinear kernel inside its tile. A procedural slide with hash noise at the finest level proved every pixel against an oracle at 2x, 4x, 8x and a fractional zoom (0 of 480000 wrong, 0 of 22144 to 43776 border pixels wrong). Black and white coarse tiles stayed pure at zoom -1.2 and -1.3, so nothing bleeds. On CMU-1.svs every 4 x 4 block of a view centred on a tile corner is uniform (0 of 30000 mixed). The negative control `interpolation: "linear"` fails all of them (479964 of 480000 pixels wrong, 30000 of 30000 blocks mixed). Only the `viv-cmu-1-svs-40x` golden changed (97.3% of pixels differ by any amount, 67.9% by more than 4 in a channel), and the 80x and tile corner goldens are new. Every other golden is identical. Result: [2026-10-26-finest-level-sampling-and-context-recovery.md](results/2026-10-26-finest-level-sampling-and-context-recovery.md), [ADR 0112](adr/0112-finest-level-sampling-and-webgl-context-recovery.md) (Proposed).
- VIEW-034 (GPU error handling) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. luma.gl never cancels `webglcontextlost`, so a lost context was never restored. `createGpuGuard` (`packages/viewer-engine/src/viv/gpu-recovery.ts`) cancels the loss, waits for the restore and has the Viv route replace its canvas and Deck from its own state (camera, slide, levels, decode reader, overlays). A forced loss with `WEBGL_lose_context` on CMU-1.svs with an overlay redrew a frame identical to the one before (0 differing pixels) with the same camera and overlay, 0 errors and one live Deck. Camera moves during the loss are drawn after the restore, a loss while the loop sleeps wakes it, and three losses in a row recover. A fourth loss within 30 s, or no restore within 5 s of visible time, reports one `GpuUnrecoverableError` with a `reason` and a `userMessage` through `onError`, releases the GPU, resolves `whenIdle` and leaves later route calls harmless. `route.canvas` and `route.deck` became getters, so other code must read them at use time. `watchWebGpuDevice` and `withErrorScopes` in `src/viv/webgpu-errors.ts` are unit-tested on a fake device and ready for VIEW-037 (deck.gl 9.4 WebGPU route), and no WebGPU route was built. A real hidden tab and a hardware GPU were not tested, and VIEW-052 (hidden tab check in headed Chrome) now covers them. Questions for the owner: the wording of `userMessage`, the 5 second restore timeout and the limit of 3 losses in 30 s.
- VIEW-024 (coordinate systems and units) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `src/camera/units.ts` in `packages/viewer-engine` converts level-0 pixels to microns and back, screen points to microns, microns per screen pixel and per device pixel, and view width in microns. It derives the effective magnification at a zoom and the scan objective (a declared value, else the nearest preset within a factor of 1.25 of the native magnification, else none), the digital flag above the scan objective and a scale bar length helper. Every function takes the microns per pixel and returns `null` when it is unknown, so an unknown spacing never gets an invented objective or digital label. `test/units.test.ts` covers slides at 0.25, 0.499, 0.2263 and unknown microns per pixel. Surprise: the 0.2263 slide is digital at 42x by the objective rule although it is below its native 44x.
- VIEW-035 (model inference pause during camera motion) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. A framework-free `InteractionSignal` (`src/engine/interaction-signal.ts`, exposed as `engine.interaction`) with an injected clock is pulsed by the engine on every camera change and by the input binding on pan and zoom, and held while a pointer gesture is active. A job calls `whenRested()` or `subscribe`, and the rest time is 200 ms. The signal arms a timer only while something listens, so an idle engine still holds zero timers. A Chrome 155 test pans for 3000 ms against a stand-in busy job (40 ms slices, not a real model): 60.0 fps with no job, 24.9 to 25.1 fps with 75 to 76 frames over 33 ms with an unpaused job, and 60.0 fps with a paused job, which resumed 280 to 285 ms after the last pan step ([result](results/2026-10-26-interaction-pause.md), [ADR 0113](adr/0113-interaction-signal-units-and-touch-gestures.md), Proposed). The Done-when line now says the number is repeated with the real region pass once a browser inference task exists. Surprise: the first measured frame of a fresh page can take 283 ms, so the harness pans 500 ms before measuring.
- VIEW-023 (touch navigation) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. The gesture interpreter recognises taps from timestamped touch pointers. A double tap zooms to 40x about the tap through the objective presets (one octave when the slide has no microns per pixel). A two-finger double tap flies to the tissue-aware whole-slide fit through the new `engine.flyToFit`. The values live in `touchTuning` (`src/input/touch.ts`): tap slop 10 px, tap length 300 ms, double-tap window 350 ms and 40 px, and a 500 ms guard against the `dblclick` Chrome fires after a touch double tap. The POC has no tap logic, so these are picks and not measurements, and the new task VIEW-053 (touch tap values on real hardware) tunes them. One-finger pan with coast and pinch with midpoint pan already existed and are now covered. Built as obvious behaviour: a touchcancel that forgets pending taps and ends a drag with no coast, and a no-jump hand-over when one pinch finger lifts. 15 unit tests and 14 Playwright tests use `hasTouch` and CDP `Input.dispatchTouchEvent`. Surprise: in CDP, `touchEnd` lists the points that lift, not the ones that stay.
- VIEW-001 (engine package API), VIEW-003 (PixelSource interface and registry), VIEW-005 (native JPEG decode with per-file validation), VIEW-006 (shared decode cache across channels), VIEW-026 (overlay layer system), VIEW-030 (memory and teardown correctness), VIEW-032 (idle means zero work), VIEW-033 (hidden tab and background scheduling) and VIEW-048 (open fitted to the tissue) ticked after green CI on `main` (runs 40189424786, 40190479158 and 40190872846). VIEW-030's polygon indices item was built with VIEW-026. VIEW-021 (wheel, trackpad and pinch handling) stays open until real device traces are recorded.
- STACK-006 (viewer hot-path state outside React), STACK-015 (Viv and deck.gl versions) and STACK-016 (TIFF and Zarr readers in the browser) ticked after green CI on `main`, with their decision records [0100](adr/0100-viewer-hot-path-state-outside-react.md), [0098](adr/0098-viv-and-deck-gl-versions.md) and [0099](adr/0099-tiff-and-zarr-readers-in-the-browser.md) still Proposed. VIEW-000 (research check for the viewer stack) ticked with them, because all three are confirmed and ADR 0098 records the Viv and deck.gl versions in use.

## 2026-10-25

- VIEW-042 (camera bounds at the slide edges), VIEW-043 (drags that always end) and VIEW-045 (camera feel profiles) ticked after green CI on `main` (runs 40023008100 and 40023008064). VIEW-021 (wheel, trackpad and pinch handling) stays open until real device traces are recorded.
- Agent guide: finished tasks are now committed and pushed in the same turn, with files staged by name, so no work lives only on one machine.
- Three agents started in parallel worktrees on VIEW-026 (overlay layer system), VIEW-032 (idle means zero work) with VIEW-033 (hidden tab and background scheduling), and VIEW-005 (native JPEG decode) with VIEW-006 (shared decode cache) and VIEW-048 (open fitted to the tissue). Their changes are merged into `main` one at a time.
- VIEW-032 (idle means zero work) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. The Viv route now gives deck.gl's animation loop a frame provider (`packages/viewer-engine/src/viv/deck-sleep.ts`, through luma.gl 9.4's `animationFrameProvider`) that parks the loop after 3 frames with nothing left to do. It wakes on a camera or slide change, every finished tile read, a canvas resize in device pixels and a pixel ratio change, and overlay changes wake it through the route's `changed()`. A poll from `whenIdle` that finds work left counts as a rescue, and the tests assert 0. On CMU-1.svs a loaded, untouched viewer ran 0 animation frame callbacks, 0 timers, 0 layouts and 0 ms of script over 10 s in all 6 runs, against 605 to 606 callbacks with the sleep switched off. The page's own main-thread time was 0.28 to 1.08 ms. Raw `TaskDuration` was 5.0 to 30.6 ms, because Chrome's PartitionAlloc reclaimer runs one 5 to 30 ms task per 10 s in the renderer, so the test traces the `toplevel` category and asserts on the page's share, and the Done-when line now says so. Built as obvious behaviour beyond the Done-when line: the route redraws no more synchronously while the tab is hidden, and a pixel ratio change wakes a sleeping loop. Result: [2026-10-25-idle-and-hidden-tab.md](results/2026-10-25-idle-and-hidden-tab.md), [ADR 0109](adr/0109-idle-means-zero-frames-and-hidden-tabs-keep-loading.md) (Proposed).
- VIEW-033 (hidden tab and background scheduling) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. Engine frame requests now go through `createGuardedFrames`, which races each animation frame against a 500 ms watchdog, runs frames as `MessageChannel` tasks while the tab is hidden, and restarts all pending requests under a new generation when visibility changes. Motion running when the tab hides lands on its target (`controller.settle()` now also advances a coast to its end), and motion started while hidden does not animate. The Viv route parks its deck loop while hidden but runs one task frame per finished tile read, so a load keeps advancing. With the tab hidden after 3 tile reads for 1.5 s at 100 ms RTT, the view was complete 65 to 136 ms after it returned (the first design, which parked everything, took 1090 to 1107 ms). Hidden before the first read it took 609 to 738 ms, because deck.gl issues tile requests from `setTimeout(0)`. `yieldToMain` and `createTimeSlice` slice long work with `scheduler.yield` or a `MessageChannel`, but nothing in the engine needs them yet. Headless Chrome cannot be hidden (`bringToFront`, minimize and a lifecycle freeze all left it visible), so the test page emulates a hidden tab, and the new task VIEW-052 (hidden tab check in headed Chrome) records a real run. Question for the owner: a hidden tab keeps loading the current view, which is what makes the 1 s budget reachable and is easy to reverse.
- VIEW-026 (overlay layer system) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. Overlays are now plain typed data in `packages/viewer-engine/src/overlay` (vector layers for human annotations, AI proposals, nuclei and measurements, raster layers for heatmap and coverage, and comment pins). One table, `overlayStack`, holds the z-order from coverage at the bottom to pins on top, and the constructors take each layer's order from its kind. `createOverlayController` sets, hides, fades, invalidates and removes layers through a new `OverlayRegistration.update`. The Viv route draws them with deck.gl `SolidPolygonLayer`, `PathLayer`, `ScatterplotLayer`, `BitmapLayer` and `IconLayer` through a builder exported from `@parfocal/viewer-engine/viv`. Polygon batches carry precomputed triangle indices, which finishes the open polygon indices item of VIEW-030 (memory and teardown correctness) ([ADR 0108](adr/0108-overlay-layers-as-typed-data-over-deck-gl.md), Proposed). A layer update of 1M polygons took 39 ms precomputed against 261 ms in deck.gl's own earcut, with identical pixels, and building the batch once took 273 ms ([result](results/2026-10-25-overlay-polygon-indices.md)). Eight new goldens over CMU-1.svs show each layer and the stack, and tests prove that hiding every layer draws the bare slide byte for byte, that registration order and timing do not change the frame, that opacity fades between the two, and that removing a layer destroys its models and buffers while hiding does not. Added beyond the task text: layers registered before mount and before the slide opens, `invalidate` that rebuilds one layer, `whenIdle` that waits for overlay layers, and a pin atlas drawn in code. Surprises: deck.gl with `_normalize: true` and per-vertex colours drew 3.1% of pixels differently from the precomputed layer (the cause was not confirmed), so comparisons use `_normalize: false` without indices. New tasks are VIEW-050 (polygons with holes) and VIEW-051 (triangulate large result sets off the main thread). Questions for the owner: the stack order, the default fill opacities, line widths and pin colours, which are the agent's choices, and whether hiding a very large layer should free its GPU buffers.
- VIEW-048 (open fitted to the tissue) margin changed after the owner asked to favour showing too much and to cut nothing from slides. The 5 percent margin now applies on every edge of the box instead of 2.5 percent, clipped to the slide, so a box that barely shrinks the view fits the whole slide. The camera only moves, no pixel is removed, and zooming out reaches the whole slide. The CMU-1.svs golden now uses a smaller fixed box that tests the rule, because the mask box of CMU-1.svs clips back to the whole slide. The native JPEG threshold of 3 of 255 stays, with the reasoning in [ADR 0110](adr/0110-native-jpeg-decode-and-tissue-fit.md).
- VIEW-005 (native JPEG decode with per-file validation) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. A TIFF source with 8-bit three-sample JPEG tiles now decodes one tile both through `createImageBitmap` and through geotiff before its first read, and the decode workers do it at open. The native decoder is kept for the whole file only when the mean absolute error is at most 3 of 255. `native-jpeg.ts` in `packages/viewer-engine/src/pixel-source/tiff` rebuilds headless tiles from `JPEGTables`, decodes with an `OffscreenCanvas` and falls back for a single read when a tile fails. The native decode already returns RGB, so the colour transform is applied once, and a decoder that returns raw YCbCr fails the probe. All three Aperio SVS fixtures fail the check (49 to 60 levels, and the POC's probe tile of CMU-1.svs gives 47.69, which reproduces the POC's 47.7), so the CMU-1.svs goldens are unchanged. Philips, Argos, Leica SCN, generic TIFF and CAMELYON files pass at 1.08 to 2.25 and decode 7 to 15 times faster, and Huron-1-40x.tif falls back at 5.44. `createImageBitmap` and `OffscreenCanvas` work inside the decode workers in Chrome 155 ([result](results/2026-10-25-native-jpeg-validation.md), [ADR 0110](adr/0110-native-jpeg-decode-and-tissue-fit.md), Proposed). A golden test shows every worker declining CMU-1.svs, and a route that trusts the native decoder fails all three goldens. Built as obvious behaviour: a per-read fallback, a decode report on each source, and `nativeJpeg` switches for tests. The pan test now counts 48 fetches and 49.6 MB because of the probe tile, with 0 duplicate bytes. TODO: confirm why Aperio fails and whether a corrected splice would let SVS use the native decoder, and look at Huron's JPEG headers.
- VIEW-006 (shared decode cache across channels) already holds, so no cache was built, and a test now proves it. The Viv route hands Viv one selection of one interleaved RGB array, so Viv asks for each region once. A browser test over CMU-1.svs counted 31 region reads for 31 regions fitted and 25 for 25 at 10x, with no region read twice and the same number dispatched to the six workers. A Node test shows one read per tile on the synthetic JPEG TIFF. Not ticked until CI is green on `main`.
- VIEW-048 (open fitted to the tissue) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. A slide can carry an optional level-0 tissue box (`EngineSlide.tissue`, passed through `pixelSlide`). `fitCamera` and the engine's fit on open and `fitToSlide` fit that box with a margin of 5 percent of each side, split over both edges and clipped to the slide, and fit the whole slide without a usable box. The zoom-out limit stays at half of the whole-slide fit. A new golden opens CMU-1.svs with the box `1856, 256, 42816 x 31104` taken from the ingest worker's tissue mask. That box is only 5 to 7 percent smaller than the slide, so the fit zooms in 2.3 percent and the golden shows the rule with little visible change ([ADR 0110](adr/0110-native-jpeg-decode-and-tissue-fit.md)). New tasks are VIEW-049 (deliver the tissue box to the viewer) and INGEST-027 (tissue box that ignores specks).

## 2026-10-23

- VIEW-042 (camera bounds at the slide edges) and VIEW-045 (camera feel profiles) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `cameraBounds` in `packages/viewer-engine/src/camera/camera.ts` is now the one place that holds the limits. The zoom runs from half the fitted size to 16 device pixels per level-0 pixel, which are the POC's values, with the device pixel ratio injected. The view centre stays on the slide. The engine re-clamps the camera on a resize or a slide close and fits it on a slide open. A coast that hits an edge stops on that axis, and a throw pressed into a wall does not coast into it. `CameraProfile` in `src/camera/profile.ts` holds every camera tunable, with Smooth (the POC's values and the default), Precise (no coasting and half the eases) and Instant presets. Reduced motion switches off the chosen profile's motion, and `engine.setCameraProfile` switches profiles live ([ADR 0106](adr/0106-camera-bounds-and-feel-profiles.md), Proposed). Built as obvious behaviour beyond the Done-when lines: camera inputs first advance a running motion to their own time, coasts end at a computed time, a flight ends on the frame its sample equals the target, and a camera change inside the engine's frame no longer requests an extra frame. A new smoothness suite (`test/camera-smoothness.test.ts`, 45 tests) plays 15 input scripts under each preset at 30, 60 and 120 Hz and with jittery frame times against a run at 1 ms frames. It checks frame-to-frame jumps, overshoot, the point under the cursor, end states, idle frames, finite values on 1 px, 200k px and zero-size cases, and drift at the limits. It found a NaN in the flight path for a far zoom-out with a short pan, now computed with `asinh`, a coast end that moved up to 0.25 px with the frame rate, and idle frames at the end of flights and against walls. Five of six bugs put back on purpose failed the suite, and the sixth, a sudden change of speed, is out of its reach. During the merge the wheel and pinch rates of VIEW-021 (wheel, trackpad and pinch handling) moved from `src/input/wheel.ts` into the profile, so `bindViewerInput` reads them live from the engine. TODO: check the Precise values and the half-octave cap with a mouse and a trackpad.
- VIEW-021 (wheel, trackpad and pinch handling) and VIEW-043 (drags that always end) implemented, not ticked. VIEW-021 waits for recorded traces and both wait for CI to be green on `main`. `bindViewerInput` in `packages/viewer-engine/src/input/dom-input.ts` maps pointer, wheel and double-click events onto the engine through a pure gesture interpreter (`gestures.ts`) and pure wheel maths (`wheel.ts`) ([ADR 0107](adr/0107-viewer-input-binding.md), Proposed). Wheel zooms by default, a pinch is told apart from Ctrl+wheel by its fractional or small delta, and each event is capped at half an octave. Middle-drag and Space+drag (a `panModifierHeld` hook until CMD-001 (key registry) exists) pan in any tool. The `auto` wheel mode guesses mouse or trackpad with the POC's rule plus mapbox-gl-js's Mac mouse step and tiny-delta rules. Shift+wheel pans sideways in pan mode, and double-click and Alt+double-click zoom one octave unless a `hitsEditableShape` hook claims the click. Built as obvious behaviour: drags end on release, cancel, lost capture, window blur, a hidden page and a mouse move with no button held, with window-level listeners and capture only attempted. Coalesced pointer samples reach the controller with their own times (`dragTo` gained an optional time). 50 new unit tests include replays of every trace file against the real camera controller, and 18 new Playwright tests drive real Chrome input over CDP and check anchoring, frame jumps, every VIEW-043 release case with and without capture, and an idle loop afterwards. The three trace files are synthetic, modelled on documented Chrome behaviour, and named `*.synthetic.json`. TODO: record a mouse, a Mac trackpad and a Windows precision touchpad with `pnpm --filter @parfocal/viewer-engine traces:record` (package README). Surprise: the POC's cap of 0.45 was in natural-log units, so it allowed 0.65 octaves, more than the backlog's half octave.
- VIEW-002 (Viv route on WebGL2 for RGB brightfield), VIEW-004 (decode worker pool) and VIEW-020 (camera controller) ticked after green CI on `main` (runs 39948001413 and 40019016940). VIEW-030 (memory and teardown correctness) stays open on its polygon indices item.
- Backlog: camera behaviour pass after the POC review. Added VIEW-045 (camera feel profiles, one live profile for every tunable with Smooth, Precise and Instant presets), VIEW-046 (objective detents on wheel zoom, an optional setting with its hold length left as a TODO for a manual check), VIEW-047 (view history with back and forward) and VIEW-048 (open fitted to the tissue mask). VIEW-022 (keyboard navigation) gained a smooth held-key pan, VIEW-043 (drags that always end) gained coalesced pointer events, and SET-006 (navigation and devices section) gained the camera feel preset and its overrides.
- Backlog and agent guide: went through the POC camera code and docs 06, 16 and 34 for viewer behaviour it already solved that the backlog did not cover. Added VIEW-042 (camera bounds at the slide edges, the POC's zoom range of half the fitted size to 16 device pixels per level-0 pixel and a centre kept on the slide), VIEW-043 (drags that always end, from doc 06 §15) and VIEW-044 (prefetch where the view is heading, from the POC's motion predictor). VIEW-021 (wheel, trackpad and pinch handling) gained the auto wheel mode, Shift+wheel and double-click zoom, and VIEW-033 (hidden tab and background scheduling) gained the camera settle in a hidden tab. ADR 0104 now names cubic ease in and out as the flight default and points its edge TODO at VIEW-042. AGENTS.md has a new working rule to build obvious behaviour as part of a task, using the POC's lessons, instead of leaving it as a TODO for the owner.
- VIEW-004 (decode worker pool) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. The Viv route now decodes in six workers by default (`decodeWorkers`, 0 decodes on the page). Each worker opens the slide with our PixelSource and returns one interleaved RGB tile as a transferred buffer, so decode, YCbCr conversion and the box average leave the main thread. Workers fetch through one 1 MiB block cache on the page (`src/decode/range-cache.ts`), which shares in-flight blocks and never aborts a download, and `createPixelSlideOpener` opens the page's own source through it. The pool (`src/decode/decode-pool.ts`) starts the queued job nearest the camera centre, drops queued jobs whose callers left, lets running jobs finish and keeps up to 32 late results for the next request ([ADR 0105](adr/0105-decode-workers-behind-one-byte-cache.md), Proposed). A Node test reproduces the POC regression of doc 06 note 11: a caller re-asking every 16 ms over a 60 ms proxy got 160 fetches and no finished tile when cancelling cancelled the download, and 1 fetch and all 4 tiles in 6 frames through the pool. A Chrome pan across CMU-1.svs at 0, 100 and 200 ms RTT took 45 to 47 fetches and 48,577,731 bytes with no byte downloaded twice ([result](results/2026-10-23-decode-pool-pan-fetches.md)). Surprise: the VIEW-002 (Viv route) path did not storm either, because Viv does not re-ask every frame, and the 1 MiB blocks, not the pool, fix the byte count. The CMU-1.svs goldens now render through the pool and still match. TODO: the over-read from 1 MiB blocks and the longest main-thread frame with and without workers are not measured yet.
- VIEW-020 (camera controller) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `packages/viewer-engine/src/camera/controller.ts` runs inside the engine's frame, before camera subscribers are notified, with the clock injected as the engine's `now` option. It pans by screen pixels, zooms about the cursor with the point under it fixed on every frame, coasts after a fast release with the POC's 130 ms decay integrated exactly, and flies along the van Wijk and Nuij zoom and pan path in 400 to 700 ms by path length, landing exactly on the target ([ADR 0104](adr/0104-camera-controller-motion.md), Proposed). Zoom limits are applied only by `clampZoom` and `clampCamera`, and every target is clamped before an anchor or path is derived from it, which avoids the POC's sideways drift. Objective presets 1.25x to 40x live in `magnification.ts`. Any new camera call, a pointer down or a wheel event cancels a flight or coast, reduced motion turns them into cuts, and unmounting lands a flight on its target. 44 new unit tests cover the physics, limits, presets, cancellation and a flight between two areas through the engine. TODO: the kit names no easing for camera flights, so cubic ease in and out is a placeholder. DOM input binding is left to VIEW-021 (wheel, trackpad and pinch handling).
- VIEW-002 (Viv route on WebGL2 for RGB brightfield) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `@parfocal/viewer-engine/viv` adds a `RendererRoute` that feeds Viv 0.23's `MultiscaleImageLayer` from our PixelSource: a virtual power-of-two ladder over the stored 1x, 4x and 16x levels, box-averaged and interleaved RGB tiles with photometric 2 (the source already converted YCbCr), white glass for absent tiles, Viv's abort sentinel, a controlled view state with Viv's own controller off, and a `whenIdle()` settle signal ([ADR 0102](adr/0102-viv-route-feeds-a-virtual-rgb-ladder.md), Proposed). Golden images of the fitted, 10x and 40x views of CMU-1.svs on SwiftShader were byte-identical over five runs, Metal stayed inside the JPEG tolerance, and routes with red and blue swapped, YCbCr handed over as RGB, or the 4x level read as 2x all failed ([result](results/2026-10-23-viv-route-goldens.md)). A new `viewer` fixture group holds CMU-1.svs, and the `browser` CI job now fetches the `small` and `viewer` groups under one cache key and fails instead of skipping when either is missing. The route releases its deck with `disposeDeck` from VIEW-030. The harness bundle seems to carry geotiff 2 from Viv's loaders next to geotiff 3. TODO: confirm and drop it, and check that the M4-written goldens pass on the x86 runner ([knowledge/viewer-build.md](knowledge/viewer-build.md)).
- VIEW-030 (memory and teardown correctness) implemented, not ticked. `src/viv/dispose-deck.ts` applies the ADR 0098 release steps and also releases a device that arrives after its deck was disposed, and `src/viv/deck-settings.ts` holds MSAA off, picking off, stable selections per slide and an r8unorm upload for uint8 channels, which draws within 1 level of Viv's own `r32float` copy ([ADR 0103](adr/0103-memory-settings-and-test-baseline.md), Proposed). A browser memory test in `packages/viewer-engine/browser` opens 26 slides through the VIEW-001 engine and a real Deck on SwiftShader: exactly one Deck alive after every visit, none after dispose, and the heap 3.9% to 4.0% over a baseline taken after one warm-up cycle, against 12.7% from the very first open, which heap snapshots put mostly in V8 compiled code. Without `canvasContext.destroy()` all 26 decks stayed alive and the heap grew 137% ([result](results/2026-10-23-memory-teardown-swiftshader.md)). This closes the heap part of VIEW-001 (engine package API) and the SwiftShader TODO of STACK-015 (Viv and deck.gl versions). It cannot be ticked yet because precomputed polygon indices belong to overlays, which do not exist, so the owner should move that item to the overlay task or leave VIEW-030 open. The test route has its own copy of the virtual ladder, which can switch to the VIEW-002 route in a follow-up.
- Started the viewer work that every draft thesis assignment shares (POC `thesis-research/analysis/report.md`, drafts A, B and C all keep the rendering comparison word for word). Four agents worked in parallel worktrees on STACK-015, STACK-016 with VIEW-003, STACK-006 with VIEW-001, and TEST-005 with TILES-006, and the changes were merged into one tree. Their ADRs 0098 to 0101 are Proposed and wait for the owner.
- STACK-015 (Viv and deck.gl versions and the WebGL2 versus WebGPU question) decided, ADR Proposed. Viv 0.23.0 now peers on deck.gl `~9.4.0`, so it runs on deck.gl 9.4.0 and luma.gl 9.4.2 with one copy of each, and a deck.gl 9.4 WebGPU route can share that install instead of the POC's nested copy. A spike opened CMU-1.svs in Viv on these versions and passed the 20-slide-switch memory test in headless Chrome 155 on a real GPU: one `Deck` alive after every switch, none after closing, and the heap 3.7% to 4.4% over baseline. Without `canvasContext.destroy()` all 21 decks stayed alive and the heap grew sevenfold, so doc 24's leak is still upstream and gets a dispose wrapper. The debug-string cost is fixed upstream from luma.gl 9.4.2, while 9.4.1 still built it 2,132 times, so VIEW-031 (luma.gl debug-string cost patch) needs no patch. [ADR 0098](adr/0098-viv-and-deck-gl-versions.md) keeps Viv on WebGL2 and deck.gl WebGPU as the alternate route ([result](results/2026-10-23-viv-version-spike.md), [knowledge/viewer-build.md](knowledge/viewer-build.md)). The spike code stayed in a temporary scratch folder outside the repository, and the result file holds the method and the dispose code. TODO: the same memory test on SwiftShader in hosted CI, under VIEW-030 (memory and teardown correctness).
- STACK-016 (TIFF and Zarr readers in the browser) decided, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0099](adr/0099-tiff-and-zarr-readers-in-the-browser.md) keeps geotiff.js 3.0.5 and zarrita 0.7.5 behind our own PixelSource and lists nine workarounds, each with a fixture and a test that shows the raw library failing. Two new committed fixtures, `synthetic-big-endian.tiff` and `synthetic-sparse.tiff`, cover big-endian offsets and absent tiles. geotiff reads deferred tag arrays of big-endian files in the wrong byte order, and it never follows SubIFDs. The POC's SPA-fallback trouble with zarrita is fixed upstream and is kept as a regression test ([knowledge/slide-formats.md](knowledge/slide-formats.md)).
- VIEW-003 (PixelSource interface and registry) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `packages/viewer-engine/src/pixel-source` defines `PixelSource` (levels, tile size, microns per pixel, photometric mode, dtype, channels, `getTile` and `getRaster` with explicit absent tiles) and a registry keyed by the ingest format ids, with an optional slide index until INGEST-007 (sidecar tile index) exists. NDPI, BIF, MIRAX and DICOM are refused for now. The conformance suite runs against the geotiff source on the committed fixtures and the cached OpenSlide and CAMELYON16 slides, whose level tables equal the ingest worker's, and against the zarrita source on generated OME-Zarr 0.4 and 0.5 stores. Leica SCN already opens the main image through the generic source, and Philips-style files have no pixel size in the browser until the slide index carries it.
- STACK-006 (viewer hot-path state outside React) decided, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. A spike panned CMU-1.svs in Viv 0.22.1 on deck.gl 9.3.11 (the POC's versions) at 60 fps, cold and warm, at 1x and 4x CPU throttle, while a React inspector showed the zoom and rendered 0 times, counted with `<Profiler onRender>` in a profiling build. An inspector reading the whole camera rendered once per camera change, 120 to 180 times in 3 s ([result](results/2026-10-23-hot-path-state-spike.md)). [ADR 0100](adr/0100-viewer-hot-path-state-outside-react.md) keeps the camera in the framework-free engine, read through `useSyncExternalStore` with a selector, with subscribers told at most once per frame. A fast cold pan at 4x fell to 24 to 32 fps whether or not React rendered, which is tile loading in the spike's minimal source ([knowledge/viewer-build.md](knowledge/viewer-build.md)).
- VIEW-001 (engine package API) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `createViewerEngine` in `packages/viewer-engine` mounts on a container, opens slides through an injected opener (aborting and closing superseded opens), sets and fits the camera, notifies camera subscribers at most once per frame, sends pointer events in slide coordinates, registers overlays in order and disposes. The renderer route is injected behind a small interface until VIEW-036 (renderer interface and route switching). Unit tests with a fake renderer and source cover the contract, and a test of 20 mount and unmount cycles leaves no listeners, resize observers, frames or routes. In Chrome the host had 0 listeners and 0 canvases after 20 remounts. The memory test from VIEW-030 (memory and teardown correctness) does not exist yet, so the heap part is still open.
- TEST-005 (golden-image rendering tests) done, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `packages/golden-images` renders a synthetic H&E-like tile with a WebGL2 reference renderer in four views, reads the frame back with `readPixels` or an element screenshot, rejects empty, uniform or transparent frames and compares the rest per channel with per-codec tolerances ([ADR 0101](adr/0101-golden-image-comparison.md), Proposed). Frames are always rendered on SwiftShader, locally too, and the suite fails on any other WebGL backend. With the shader edited to swap red and blue, all four golden tests failed and named the swap. SwiftShader frames were identical across runs and Metal differed by at most 1 level ([result](results/2026-10-23-golden-image-tolerances.md)). A new `browser` CI job under `ci-ok` runs `pnpm test:browser`, and `pnpm golden:update` rewrites the goldens. TODO: check on the first CI run that goldens written on an Apple M4 pass on the x86 runner, and tune the JPEG and JPEG 2000 tolerances when the Viv route decodes real fixtures.
- TILES-006 (latency proxy test harness) done, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `packages/latency-proxy` adds a set RTT to every request and paces downloads to a shared bandwidth limit, configurable from code or through `/__latency`. A Playwright fixture makes one project per RTT, so perf specs run at 0, 50, 100 and 200 ms. Measured added latency was the set RTT plus 1 to 2 ms and bandwidth held within 3 ms of the expected time ([result](results/2026-10-23-latency-proxy.md)). Timing tests assert on the fastest of several samples, because the Python suites running alongside under `pnpm test` added up to 14 ms. Twelve parallel requests take two RTTs because Chrome opens six HTTP/1.1 connections. TODO: an HTTP/2 mode if a TEST-006 (performance budget suite) budget needs more than six requests in flight.

## 2026-10-20

- The report templates now follow the owner's ask for a page you put blocks on, like the clinical report idea (POC doc 43). The evaluation report shows as its pages with a PDF preview, the template editor and Arrange are one page editor with drag and drop, block settings and a page and format tab, and New template asks for the name, kind, starting point and paper. Edited EVAL-002, EVAL-008, EVAL-009 and ADMIN-008 and added EVAL-010 (PDF preview).
- Supervisor feedback from the meeting on 2026-10-20, recorded in POC doc 43. Admin and researcher are joined: a workspace has researchers and reviewers, and admin rights are a switch on a researcher that opens the admin page and adds no data access ([ADR 0096](adr/0096-researcher-and-reviewer-with-admin-rights.md), superseding ADR 0061, with each role's responsibilities written out). The evaluation report is built from blocks, and study templates preset a review task and its report ([ADR 0097](adr/0097-report-templates-built-from-blocks.md)). New tasks are IAM-024 (admin as a right of a researcher), ADMIN-008 (report template library), EVAL-007 (study template model and API), EVAL-008 (report from template blocks), EVAL-009 (arrange one report) and TASK-009 (study template in a new review task), and ADMIN-001, ADMIN-003, ONB-004, PROJ-002, PROJ-008, SET-011, JOBS-005, JOBS-006, HOME-009, INGEST-026, EVAL-002, EVAL-003 and ADJ-004 were edited. The thesis chapters the supervisor asked for, with motivation, state of the art, one proposed solution chapter and a technical part, are in the thesis plan (since 2026-10-31 in the private repository `fedasevich/parfocal-thesis`). Surprise from the POC thesis data: a quarter of application theses put design and implementation in one chapter with no score difference, but only 5 to 7% also put testing there.

The upload has three steps, with results listed under their slides and Match folded into Files (INGEST-021, CASES-016, CASES-024, RSET-004). NAV-001 folds the magnification bar to a chip, ADMIN-002 and ADMIN-006 show space as a split bar per kind and per project. New tasks are VIEW-041 (how a slide loads), GUIDE-010 (notes on decisions), GUIDE-011 (optional and several reasons, reasons on Unsure, amending ADR 0072 after GUIDE-001 was built with a required reason) and IAM-023 (sign in and unlock with a passkey).

## 2026-10-18

- CASES-009 (search API) is not ticked. On the hosted CI runner its latency test on 100,000 cases gave a median of 102 ms and a p95 of 118 ms, over the 100 ms budget, while the same test passed locally. The test now runs only with `PARFOCAL_PERF=1`. TODO: make case search meet the budget on slower machines (ADR 0094 option 2) or settle where the budget is measured.
- The code of CASES-009 (search API) went into the TEST-016 and STACK-034 tick commit (`eefa404`) by mistake, because it was staged when the tick ran, so the commit named for CASES-009 (`69e0557`) holds only its log line and claim. The tick helper now refuses to run while anything is staged.
- CASES-009 (search API) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `GET /api/search` finds projects, cases, slides and people by part of a name, objects by any part of their path, and patients by whole words, answering a patient match with its cases and never the name. Everything is read through the project policies, so reviewers find only their task slides and other workspaces nothing. API tests cover each kind, the object path forms, the patient rule and the scopes, and a latency test on 100,000 seeded cases keeps p95 under the 100 ms of STACK-034 ([knowledge/api.md](knowledge/api.md)).
- Ticked TEST-016 (test data hygiene) and STACK-034 (search) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- Ticked TEST-002 (API test harness) and TEST-003 (tenant isolation test generator) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- STACK-034 (search) decided, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. A spike on 100,000 synthetic cases, 240,000 slides and 100,000 patients answered case searches in 27 ms, slides in 37 ms and whole-word patients in 10 ms at p50, all under the 100 ms budget ([result](results/2026-10-18-search-spike.md)). Under row-level security the trigram index is never used, because `ILIKE` is not leakproof, so the scan grows with the table. [ADR 0094](adr/0094-search-on-postgres.md) keeps search in Postgres under the policies, with no trigram index, and reopens the choice above 300,000 cases in a workspace ([knowledge/database.md](knowledge/database.md)).
- TEST-016 (test data hygiene) done, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. Patient names used by tests now come from a registry of synthetic names (`tests/synthetic_people.py`), and a scanner fails on any patient name in the test folders or their fixtures that is not registered. It reads names given to `PatientIdentity`, in patient bodies of API calls and in the name column of case import CSVs, and a second test shows it finds a name in each of those places. The eleven names in use today are registered ([knowledge/test-fixtures.md](knowledge/test-fixtures.md)).
- Ticked MLEVAL-004 (typing and calibration evaluation) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- TEST-003 (tenant isolation test generator) done, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. The isolation suite already failed for an unlisted GET route or an unseeded table. A new test now sends all 100 guarded and member calls with one workspace's ids as each role of another workspace and checks that no row of the first workspace's tenant tables changed and that no call answers 5xx. A new route must be listed for the permission suite anyway, so it joins this check automatically. Sent by the first workspace's own members, the same calls do change its rows, which shows the test can fail ([knowledge/api.md](knowledge/api.md)).
- TEST-002 (API test harness) done, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. The harness already had a migrated database per test run, factories for workspaces, people and projects and a client per principal. `make_case` and `make_slide` now complete the factories, and `tests/test_harness.py` uses every factory, a client for each role and a signed-in browser in one test ([knowledge/api.md](knowledge/api.md)).
- Ticked MLEVAL-000 (research check) and MLEVAL-001 (dataset registry and loaders) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- MLEVAL-004 (typing and calibration evaluation) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `parfocal_ml.type_evaluation` gives accuracy, balanced accuracy, recall, precision, the confusion matrix, a temperature fitted on another fold, ECE before and after and the confidence bands. On the POC's cached HoVer-NeXt outputs for PanNuke fold 3 it reproduces every number of POC doc 29 for one view and for eight views ([result](results/2026-10-18-typing-calibration-reproduction.md)). Unit tests cover hand-worked cases, and the reproduction test runs where the cached outputs are present ([knowledge/evaluation.md](knowledge/evaluation.md)).
- Ticked MLEVAL-002 (geometric scorer) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- MLEVAL-001 (dataset registry and loaders) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `parfocal_ml.datasets` registers the seven evaluation datasets of ADR 0093 with their licences, downloads TNBC v1.1 and NuInsSeg from Zenodo, checks size and MD5 against the published values, and loads samples straight from the archives. NuInsSeg loads 665 images of 31 organs and 30,698 nuclei, equal to the POC per image. TNBC v1.1 loads 50 images with 4,053 nuclei, three fewer than the POC's copy, which matches no published version ([result](results/2026-10-18-dataset-loaders.md)). Tests check the checksum refusal and the registry everywhere, and the sample counts where the archives are cached. The other datasets need a sign-in or have no published checksum, so their loaders moved to a new task, MLEVAL-010 (loaders for datasets behind a sign-in) ([knowledge/evaluation.md](knowledge/evaluation.md)).
- MLEVAL-000 (research check for model evaluation) done, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0093](adr/0093-evaluation-metrics-and-datasets.md) fixes the metrics per task (PQ and AJI, mIoU and NoC, accuracy and ECE, FROC and Dice) and lists the datasets with their roles and licences from their own pages: MoNuSeg, PanNuke and Lizard under CC BY-NC-SA 4.0, TNBC and NuInsSeg under CC BY 4.0, CAMELYON16 under CC0. CryoNuSeg's licence could not be found, and Lizard's comes from a derived record, so both are TODOs. Datasets stay outside the repository and are used for evaluation, and training on the non-commercial ones for a paying tenant waits for the owner.
- Ticked NOTIF-005 (digest and quiet hours) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- MLEVAL-002 (geometric scorer) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `parfocal_ml.scoring` scores predicted rings against labelled nuclei with 4x supersampled centre sampling and gives recall, precision, F1, PQ, SQ, AJI and per-group breakdowns. On the POC's TNBC predictions it gives exactly the POC scorer's numbers for all 11 methods, and it showed that two of the POC's stored numbers are stale ([result](results/2026-10-18-geometric-scorer-reproduction.md)). Unit tests cover hand-worked cases, and a reproduction test runs where the POC fixtures are present. `workers/ml` now depends on numpy, scipy and scikit-image ([knowledge/evaluation.md](knowledge/evaluation.md)).
- Ticked NOTIF-004 (email templates without PHI) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- NOTIF-005 (digest and quiet hours) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. People choose immediate, hourly or daily email and quiet hours in their own time zone on the user row (migration `0038`), through `GET` and `PUT /api/me/email-schedule` ([ADR 0092](adr/0092-email-digest-and-quiet-hours.md)). Delivery holds emails during quiet hours, batches them for the digest window, sends a single waiting email on its own and several as one digest linking to the inbox, and never holds emergency access. Tests cover quiet hours across midnight with a mandatory email going through, an hourly digest, a daily digest with one item and the settings validation ([knowledge/api.md](knowledge/api.md)).
- Ticked NOTIF-006 (event catalogue with deep links) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- NOTIF-004 (email templates without PHI) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `parfocal_api.email_templates` renders the invitation and every notification kind from fixed text with three allowed values (the recipient's name, the workspace name and the link), as ADR 0039 asked, and the invitation and notification senders now pass the rendered subject, text and HTML. Tests render every template with sentinel values and check that only those values appear, that no patient sentinel can, that every email links to the app and that names are escaped in HTML. An allow-list test pins the templates and their parameters. The notification catalogue moved to `parfocal_api.notification_catalogue` to break an import cycle ([knowledge/api.md](knowledge/api.md)).
- Consolidated memory a third time on 2026-10-18, after about 25 log entries. "Where we are now" and "Next up" in `docs/README.md` were rewritten around the Home, notification and security backends and the cloud-only TODOs they left. The roadmap's M3 line lists the new backend pieces. Risk R17 (security controls tested only locally, with their cloud parts as TODOs) is new, and R12 now lists ADRs up to 0091. The new lessons are in `knowledge/api.md`, `knowledge/auth.md` and `knowledge/database.md`, and the `ApiAs` lesson now says it keeps recurring. Every ticked task has a log entry, every ADR and result file is indexed, and ADR 0052 (the task board) is still Proposed while in use.
- Ticked CASES-020 (next slide in a task) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- NOTIF-006 (event catalogue with deep links) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. Every notification kind now has a catalogue entry with its target kind, a subject, a plain line and its default channels, and `task-due-soon`, `result-set-added` and `integration-down` are new (migration `0037`). Emails link to `/go/{notification id}`, and `GET /api/me/notifications/{id}/target` resolves the target for its recipient: a task, an evaluation, a job, a project or a slide with its task and object ([ADR 0091](adr/0091-notification-catalogue-and-targets.md)). Thread and delivery targets wait for COLLAB-002 and REMOTE-003 and answer 409 until then. Tests check the catalogue and resolve every kind that has a target today, and refuse other people's notifications and targets out of reach ([knowledge/api.md](knowledge/api.md)).
- Ticked SEC-007 (right to access and erasure workflows) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- CASES-020 (next slide in a task) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `GET /api/slides/{slide}/neighbours` returns the previous and next slide, the position, the total and whether the slide is the last, for a task (skipping the caller's submitted slides unless asked not to), for My work's All slides with its filters, or for a list of ids the client sends, which covers search results until the search API exists. API tests cover each list kind, skipping, a split task, the end of the list and the refusals, and the route joins the permission and isolation suites ([knowledge/api.md](knowledge/api.md)).
- SEC-007 (access and erasure workflows) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. The owner chose to erase a patient while keeping their cases, and to pseudonymise a person while keeping their work ([ADR 0090](adr/0090-access-export-and-erasure.md)). `GET /api/me/personal-data` exports every row that names the signed-in person in each of their workspaces, found from the table models, with their profile, sessions, invitations and audit entries. Project owners export a patient's identifiers, cases and slides and erase the patient, which unlinks the cases and is refused under a legal hold. Platform operators erase a person: "Former member" in place of the profile, a new subject, the pseudonym forgotten, sessions ended, and only the memberships nothing points at removed. Tests cover each workflow end to end, a guard test fails when a personal column is not exported, and the routes join the permission and isolation suites ([knowledge/api.md](knowledge/api.md)).
- Ticked SEC-006 (retention policies) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- SEC-006 (retention policies) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. Migration `0036` adds `retention_policies` and `legal_holds` and the `SECURITY DEFINER` function `parfocal_retention_purge()`, the only way to hard-delete, which purges expired soft-deleted annotations, result sets and slides unless their project is held or a review task still uses them. Old audit entries are purged as a prefix of the chain with an `audit.purged` anchor, as the owner chose, and `chain_breaks` verifies from that anchor ([ADR 0089](adr/0089-retention-purge-and-audit-re-anchor.md)). The `retention` workflow runs once a day per workspace and audits every purged record. Admins set the policy and place or release holds through `/api/tenant/retention` and `/api/tenant/legal-holds`. Tests run the scheduled job and check what it purged, kept and audited, that the chain still verifies after a purge and breaks on tampering, and that a workspace hold stops everything. Stored files, reports and cases are TODOs, because none of them can be deleted yet ([knowledge/database.md](knowledge/database.md)).
- Ticked SEC-012 (rate limiting and abuse protection) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- SEC-012 (rate limiting) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. The owner chose counters in Postgres for people and workspaces, with per-IP limits at the edge ([ADR 0088](adr/0088-rate-limits-in-postgres-and-at-the-edge.md)). `authenticate` upserts a one-minute counter per person or service account and per workspace into the UNLOGGED `rate_counters` table (migration `0035`, auth scope only) and answers 429 `rate-limited` with `Retry-After` past 600 or 3,000 a minute. A load test fires 30 concurrent requests at a limit of 10 and gets exactly 10 answers and 20 refusals, another shows two service accounts sharing the workspace limit, and a signed-in person is limited too. Problems can now carry headers. The edge part is a new task, SEC-027 (edge limits per IP), which waits for cloud work.
- Ticked SEC-016 (threat model) and SEC-025 (origin check on cookie writes) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- SEC-025 (Origin check on cookie writes) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. A middleware refuses unsafe requests that carry the session cookie and no bearer token unless they come from the app origin, by `Origin` or by `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin` ([ADR 0087](adr/0087-origin-check-on-cookie-writes.md)). It closes X1 of the threat model, a sibling subdomain that SameSite=Strict lets through. Unit tests cover the rule for each header combination, and an API test with a signed-in browser shows a write from another subdomain refused while reads and same-origin writes pass. The signed-in test browser now sends the app origin like a real one ([knowledge/auth.md](knowledge/auth.md)).
- Ticked HOME-001 (home API aggregates) and REVIEW-003 (staging computation API) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- SEC-016 (threat model) written, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [security/threat-model.md](security/threat-model.md) lists the trust boundaries and the STRIDE threats for upload, tile access, guest links, AI jobs and realtime, each rated and linked to the task that mitigates it. Two High threats had no task, so SEC-025 (Origin check on cookie writes) and SEC-026 (isolated parsing of uploaded files) are new. SameSite=Strict does not stop a sibling subdomain, which X1 records. JOBS-005 (connect a lab model), COLLAB-001 (realtime infrastructure) and REMOTE-001 (read in place) now name the address rules, sealed credentials and token expiry the model asks for. Most of these parts are still planned, so the model states the design they must meet.
- REVIEW-003 (staging computation) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `parfocal_common.node_staging.stage_node` takes a node's areas (AI size, call, measurement, nuclei) and returns the lead area, its size range, the tumor cell count range from POC doc 33's expected-mistakes method, the category, the suggested pN(sn) stage and the state open, so far or settled, with each area's highest possible category. [ADR 0086](adr/0086-node-staging-rules.md) records two departures from the mock: a measurement can change the lead, and a node settles only when no other area could still outrank the lead. The property that firm answers are never wrong needs per-nucleus labels that the POC kept only as aggregates, so it moved to a new task, REVIEW-020 (firm staging answers on a validation set). Unit tests cover each category boundary, the count range and its firm rule, a ruled-out lead, a measurement taking the lead, a rival area and a small cluster near 200 cells.
- HOME-001 (home API aggregates) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `GET /api/me/home` returns the resume target derived from my newest decision, edit or added object on an unsubmitted slide (with its position among my slides and my decisions there), my open tasks by due date with who created each, my projects with member, slide, result set and reviewed counts and a four-slide strip, the slides without results, the last seven days (objects checked, the share agreed, open disagreements for the projects I adjudicate) and the current announcements. My work's To review query moved into `my_review_tasks` so both use it, and its tasks now carry `created_by_user_id`. API tests cover each block for owners, reviewers, outsiders and admins, and the route joins the member routes of the permission suite and the isolation suite ([knowledge/api.md](knowledge/api.md)).
- Ticked HOME-011 (announcements API) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- Ticked HOME-000 (research check for Home) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- HOME-011 (announcements API) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `announcements` (migration `0034`, under row-level security) holds a kind (`model`, `slowdown` or `change`), a text of at most 280 characters, an optional end time and who posted it. `GET /api/announcements` lists the current ones newest first to every member, and `POST /api/announcements` and `POST /api/announcements/{id}/end` need the workspace settings write grant, so only admins post and end them. Ending is idempotent, an end time in the past is refused, and the audit entry carries the kind but not the text. API tests cover posting, listing for each role, expiry, ending early, validation and refusals, and the route joins the permission matrix, the member routes and the isolation suite.
- Ticked RSET-008 (export reviewed annotations) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- HOME-000 (research check for Home) done, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0085](adr/0085-home-aggregate-api.md) maps every block of the mock's home scenes to one `GET /api/me/home`: the resume target derived from my newest decision, edit or added object (the camera waits for HOME-003, resume state), my open tasks, my projects with a four-slide strip and the reviewed count, slides without results, the last seven days in numbers and the announcements. The announcements API is a new task, HOME-011, split from HOME-009, which keeps the screen. Mentions, the checklist and platform-wide announcements are left for COLLAB, ONB-006 and a later owner decision.
- RSET-008 (export reviewed annotations) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `GET /api/review-tasks/{task}/slides/{slide}/export` returns one reviewer's layer or the consensus as GeoJSON or Parfocal JSON, with full IDs, paths, decisions and what changed under `properties.parfocal`. The ingest worker gained `read_parfocal_json`, and `read_geojson` now keeps `properties.parfocal`. Round-trip tests export a reviewed layer in both formats, validate the Parfocal file against the schema, read both back and compare every object, its class, normalised geometry, changes and path. Other tests cover the consensus (agreed regions plus added objects merged across reviewers) and who may export what. Merging added objects kept whichever object had the lower UUID, so the evaluation and the export now share `first_of_each_match`, which keeps the earliest ([knowledge/results.md](knowledge/results.md)).
- Ticked EVAL-006 (task evaluation API) and RSET-002 (Parfocal JSON schema and validator) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- RSET-002 (Parfocal JSON schema and validator) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. The schema is published without its draft mark, with a template, a [reference page](reference/parfocal-results-v1.md) listing ten error codes, and copies in `parfocal_common/schemas` that a test keeps equal to the ones in `docs/reference`. `parfocal_common.results_schema` validates with `jsonschema` (Draft 2020-12, a new `py-common` dependency) and maps every problem to its line by walking the JSON text. It runs as a command line tool and behind `POST /api/result-formats/parfocal-json/validate`, with the schema and template downloadable from the same prefix. Tests accept the template and reject one example of each code on its expected line. The help drawer moved to a new screen task, RSET-010 (help drawer "What files work?").
- Consolidated memory a second time on 2026-10-18, after about 30 log entries since the first. "Where we are now" and "Next up" in `docs/README.md` were rewritten around the finished review loop backend and what waits for storage and for the UI. The roadmap shows 20 of 40 STACK slots and the M3 backend done. Risk R16 (backend split out of screen tasks may drift from the final UI) is new, and R12 now lists ADRs up to 0084. The lessons on review states, reviewer decisions, decisions on queued objects, opinions, My work and evaluation went into `knowledge/api.md` and the new `knowledge/evaluation.md`, and `knowledge/tooling.md` gained the repository typecheck and the working-tree check. Every ticked task has a log entry and every ADR and result file is indexed.
- EVAL-006 (task evaluation API) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `GET /api/review-tasks/{id}/evaluation` gathers a task's reviewer decisions, decisions on queued objects, reviewed layers, added objects merged across reviewers, region areas computed in PostGIS and opinion answers into the EVAL-001 engine, and returns precision and sensitivity per class, Dice, kappa, cell agreement, reasons and opinion summaries with their counts. An API test reproduces the values of a seeded task worked out by hand (precision 1/2 and Dice 0.4 for the regions, κ = 0.5 between the two reviewers), and reviewers and other workspaces are refused. Cells count as unclassified until their classes can be read from the chunk summary, and the Dice consensus uses the earliest saved reshape because tasks store no reviewer order ([knowledge/evaluation.md](knowledge/evaluation.md)).
- Ticked EVAL-001 (metrics engine) and GUIDE-009 (opinion answers API) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- GUIDE-009 (opinion answers API) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. The opinion answers were part of GUIDE-007 (opinion form for the reviewer), a screen, so the backend was split into this new task, and GUIDE-007 now depends on it. The task, with EVAL-006 (task evaluation API), went into the backlog in the ADJ-006 tick commit by mistake. `opinions` (migration `0033`) keeps one opinion per task, slide and reviewer on the pinned form version. `PUT` merges answers into a draft after checking each against its question, refuses unknown keys and refuses changes once the slide is submitted, and `GET` returns the questions, answers and draft flag to the reviewer and to project owners. API tests cover every question kind, the merge and removal, the pinned version after a new form is published, the lock and reopen, the roles and an audit entry without the answer text.
- EVAL-001 (metrics engine) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `parfocal_common.evaluation` computes the consensus per object, precision and sensitivity per kind and class with their counts, Dice per class over slides from areas the caller supplies, pairwise Cohen's kappa, Fleiss' kappa, cell agreement, disagreement reasons and opinion summaries, as [ADR 0084](adr/0084-evaluation-metrics.md) defines them. Unit tests reproduce the hand-computed values of a fixture task (κ = 51/87 between its two reviewers) and the textbook Fleiss example (κ ≈ 0.210). Gathering the inputs from the database is a new backend task, EVAL-006 (task evaluation API), which EVAL-002 (evaluation report page) now depends on ([knowledge/evaluation.md](knowledge/evaluation.md)).
- Ticked ADJ-006 (decisions on queued objects) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- Ticked CASES-006 (CSV import) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- ADJ-006 (decisions on queued objects) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `disagreement_decisions` (migration `0032`) holds keep the AI's call or go with the reviewers, with an optional note, per queued object. Two routes set and clear a decision for project owners, refused for objects that are not queued and after sign-off, each audited. Decided objects stay in the queue listing with their decision and leave the open count, so a task with every queued object decided can be signed off. API tests cover each decision, a change, a clear, the refusal after sign-off, the open count through the states and sign-off, the roles and the audit entries.
- EVAL-000 (research check for the evaluation report) done. [ADR 0084](adr/0084-evaluation-metrics.md) scores the AI against one consensus answer per object (unanimous decisions, else the decision on the queued object), with precision and sensitivity per class for regions and cells, Dice per slide between the AI's and the consensus regions, pairwise Cohen's kappa between reviewers (Fleiss' kappa with more than two), cell agreement, errors by reason and opinion summaries, each with its count. The decisions on queued objects move from the ADJ-002 screen into a new backend task, ADJ-006 (decisions on queued objects), which EVAL-001 (metrics engine) and ADJ-002 now depend on. The PDF choice is Typst ([ADR 0083](adr/0083-typst-for-report-pdfs.md)).
- STACK-035 (PDF generation for reports) done. [ADR 0083](adr/0083-typst-for-report-pdfs.md) picks Typst through the typst-py wheel. A [spike](results/2026-10-18-pdf-generation-spike.md) in a Linux container rendered a two-page evaluation report with a signature line and the AI disclosure in 12 to 18 ms cold and 4 ms warm with Typst, against 218 ms and 80 ms with WeasyPrint, which also needs Pango and HarfBuzz in the image. Typst's PDF was the same across containers only once its `timestamp` was fixed, so exports use the sign-off time and keep their checksum. Chromium printing and ReportLab were not run.
- CASES-006 (CSV import) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `POST /api/projects/{id}/case-imports` reads patients, cases and slide manifests from a CSV with a dry run by default, a validation report with row and column for every problem, and an idempotent re-run that finds cases by label, slides by case and label and patients by the MRN blind index. Nothing is written when any row has a problem. Tests import a 500-row file (100 cases with patients, 500 slides) twice and find no duplicates, add only new slides on a re-run, report each kind of problem by row, refuse patient fields in a de-identified project, check existing cases and barcodes, and allow only project owners. Migration `0031` adds `import` as a case source.
- Ticked ADJ-001 (disagreement detection), WL-001 (My work query API) and AINUC-003 (chunk schema with probabilities) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- AINUC-003 (chunk schema with probabilities) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `parfocal_common.cell_chunks` encodes and decodes the ADR 0076 chunk and summary container with aligned typed fields and checks it, and a chunk from a model run fails the check when its type probabilities are missing. Unit tests round-trip a chunk, fail a run chunk without probabilities and pass an imported one, and name missing, mistyped and miscounted fields.
- WL-001 (My work query API) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. Three routes follow [ADR 0082](adr/0082-my-work-query-api.md): `/api/me/review-tasks` lists my tasks with slides submitted, decisions, my status and the next slide, in-progress first and then by due date, `/api/me/assigned-tasks` lists the tasks I created with each reviewer's progress and the task status (not started, in review, disagreements, ready to sign off, signed off), and `/api/me/slides` lists every slide I can read with result sets, reviews and status, filtered by project, status, result set, reviewer and a label search, in keyset pages with an opaque cursor. API tests cover each filter, the cursor across a status filter, the statuses as a task moves to sign-off, members only and workspace isolation. The ADJ-001 run failed typecheck on a test in `py-common` that a single-package check missed, which a follow-up commit fixed ([knowledge/tooling.md](knowledge/tooling.md)).
- WL-000 (research check for My work) done. [ADR 0082](adr/0082-my-work-query-api.md) splits the My work API into three read endpoints, one per tab (To review, Assigned by me, All slides), that derive states with the existing pure functions, pages All slides by keyset with an opaque cursor, and moves the storage filter to REMOTE-001 (read in place from a connected source), because slides have no storage location yet. WL-001 and WL-017 now say so.
- ADJ-001 (disagreement detection) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. Once every reviewer of a slide has submitted, an object goes into the task's queue when its reviewers' decisions differ or when they all disagree with the AI, which is the owner's rule in [ADR 0080](adr/0080-disagreement-rule-and-sign-off.md) (more than half against the AI only adds those cases). `GET /api/review-tasks/{id}/disagreements` lists each queued object with why and every reviewer's decision for owners and viewers. The open count now drives the `disagreement-queue` state and refuses sign-off. A unit table covers both kinds and the cases that are not queued, and an API test on a seeded task queues one object of each kind, refuses sign-off, hides the queue from reviewers and empties it when a reviewer reopens.
- Ticked GUIDE-001 (decision model and API) and TASK-003 (scope and progress) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- TASK-003 (scope and progress) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `parfocal_common.scope` picks the objects in scope for each of the five scope kinds from candidates the caller supplies, samples random cells with a generator seeded by the task's seed and the slide, and counts progress as judged objects inside the scope. Unit tests on a fixture slide match the hand-counted progress of two reviewers for every scope, and prove the sample is the same for every reviewer and input order and different per slide and per task. Resolving cell scopes in the API waits for the chunk summary to be in storage.
- GUIDE-001 (decision model and API) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `verdicts` (migration `0030`) holds agree, disagree or unsure per task, slide, reviewer and AI object, with a reason from the ADR 0072 list on disagree, the right class with `wrong_class` and a note of up to 500 characters. Three routes save, clear (undo) and list decisions. A `wrong_class` decision retypes the cell in the reviewer's layer in the same transaction, and changing or clearing it takes the retype back out. Decisions are refused after sign-off until the owner reopens, every save and clear is audited without the note, and a decision puts the reviewer in review. The isolation and permission suites cover the new routes and table.
- Ticked TASK-002 (slide states per task and reviewer) after a green `ci` run on `main`. The first run failed because two API tests in `test_review_states.py` had no `db` mark and ran in the job without Postgres, which the follow-up commit fixed.
- Ticked INGEST-005 (malware and integrity scan) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- TASK-002 (slide states per task and reviewer) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `parfocal_api.review_states` derives each reviewer's and each slide's state with pure functions and checks the four stored moves (submit, reopen a review, sign off, reopen a task) for their actor, with sign-off allowed only when every slide is submitted and no disagreement is open, as the owner decided in [ADR 0080](adr/0080-disagreement-rule-and-sign-off.md). Five routes show the states and make the moves, each move audited, and sign-off is stored on the task (migration `0029`). Sign-off now locks reviewer moves and the reviewed layer, which closes the ADR 0079 TODO. A table test covers every move for every actor, a second table covers the derived states, and an API test walks a task from assigned to signed off and reopened.
- INGEST-005 (malware and integrity scan) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `parfocal_ingest.integrity` refuses a TIFF-family file whose tiles or strips run past its end and a zip with a member that fails its CRC, each with a plain reason. Tests refuse the EICAR test file under three names as not a slide, refuse a cut-off TIFF that still identifies, refuse a zip with a changed byte, and pass eight whole fixtures.
- The owner decided four open questions. ADJ-000 (research check for disagreements and sign-off) is done in [ADR 0080](adr/0080-disagreement-rule-and-sign-off.md): an object is queued when its reviewers' decisions differ or more than half disagree with the AI, and sign-off needs every slide submitted and no undecided object, as a soft lock. ADJ-001 and ADJ-004 now say so. Uploads get structure checks without an antivirus scanner ([ADR 0081](adr/0081-uploads-are-checked-by-structure.md)), because ClamAV cannot scan files above a few GB. The ID matching defaults (IoU 0.5 and 4 µm), Epithelial `#43999d` and the "Main error type" options stay as accepted defaults.
- Ticked RSET-007 (reviewed layer), OBJ-002 (IDs across result sets) and RSET-003 (readers for GeoJSON, CSV and masks) after a green `ci` run on `main` that includes all three.
- RSET-003 (readers for GeoJSON, CSV and masks) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `parfocal_ingest.results` reads GeoJSON (including QuPath exports) with msgspec and shapely, CSV and TSV through a column mapping with guessed defaults, instance and class masks traced per label with OpenCV, and heatmaps as rasters, all into one object form with problems named by feature, row or mask value. The QuPath fixture is a real QuPath 0.7.0 export made headless from `CMU-1-Small-Region.svs` with the committed script, and it settled the ADR 0070 TODO on `objectType`, `nucleusGeometry`, colours and coordinates ([knowledge/results.md](knowledge/results.md)). msgspec 0.19 silently produced empty structs on Python 3.14, so the worker pins 0.21, as the spike did, and OpenCV 5. Writing the objects into result sets waits for uploads to reach storage.
- OBJ-002 (IDs across result sets) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `parfocal_ingest.matching` matches a new set's regions to the slide's known ones by IoU of at least 0.5 and its cells by centroids within 4 µm, one to one and best first, and returns inherited, new and missing objects, with new numbers in the ADR 0071 order. shapely and scipy joined the ingest worker, which imports results until a separate results worker is needed. Tests on two fixture sets cover inherited, new and missing regions and cells, a retyped region that keeps its ID, a closest-wins tie and the pixel distance per scale.
- Ticked TASK-001 (review task model and API) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- RSET-007 (reviewed layer) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0079](adr/0079-reviewed-layer.md) stores each reviewer's retype, reshape and delete of an AI region or cell in `review_edits` per task and slide (migration `0028`), and added objects as annotations tagged with the task, numbered T⁺ and C⁺. Four routes save and undo an edit, add an object and show a layer with its comparison against the set. Only the assigned reviewer edits their layer, and a project owner reads any reviewer's. The tests snapshot the AI objects and the set before and after every kind of edit and find them unchanged. Edits after sign-off are not refused yet, because sign-off waits for the owner's ADJ-000 decision.
- Ticked OBJ-001 (ID grammar and allocation) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- TASK-001 (review task model and API) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0078](adr/0078-review-task-model.md) pins one finished result set per slide, chosen per slide or by model name, and stores assignments from the split: everyone, even (slides dealt in turn) or chosen pairs. Tasks have a per-project number shown as RT-n, a scope with its count and a fixed random seed or a score, the opinion form version, blinding, the first read without AI, the start mode, a due date and up to three reminders (migration `0027`). Four routes create, list, show and edit tasks, with audit entries. Project reviewers now read and draw on the slides of their tasks, see only those slides in a case, and list only their own tasks. Holding an `ApiAs` client across calls as another person made a test act as the owner, which is now in [knowledge/api.md](knowledge/api.md).
- OBJ-001 (ID grammar and allocation) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `parfocal_common.object_ids` writes and parses every display and ASCII form of [ADR 0071](adr/0071-object-id-grammar.md), including cells inside regions, person marks and split suffixes, and the full path with percent-encoded segments and `~` for a loose slide. `by_area` and `in_reading_order` give the allocation order, and `parfocal_api.numbering.reserve` hands out per-slide numbers from `slide_counters` for annotations and imports alike. Case labels may no longer be `~`. 31 unit tests cover the forms, malformed IDs and paths, and both orders.
- Ticked RSET-001 (result set model and API) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
