- RSET-001 (result set model and API) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0077](adr/0077-result-sets.md) creates a set as `importing`, lets the worker add regions, zones and chunk rows, then marks it `ready` with its counts or `failed` with a code. Triggers and revoked grants make a finished set immutable in the database (migration `0026`). Five routes create an imported set with its classes resolved through the project's mappings, list sets per slide and per project, show one with its classes, and soft-delete it, with audit entries for create and delete. Project owners and service accounts with `upload-slides` add sets. The confirmation before deleting a reviewed set needs review tasks, which depend on this task, so it moved to the new RSET-009 (confirm deleting a reviewed result set). The isolation and permission suites cover the new routes and the four tables.
- Ticked ANN-020 (annotation persistence API) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- Consolidated memory after 24 log entries on 2026-10-18. "Where we are now" and "Next up" in `docs/README.md` were rewritten with new rows for review data, ingest and notifications, and the decisions held for the owner are listed in one place. The roadmap shows 19 of 40 STACK slots and the M3 progress. In the risks, R3 records the ingest checks, R12 is now likely, and R15 (ingest steps built only on local files while the upload path is undecided) is new. The lessons on geometry columns, append-only annotations and review writes before tasks went into `knowledge/database.md` and `knowledge/api.md`, and the slide format lessons into `knowledge/slide-formats.md`. Every ticked task has a log entry and every ADR and result file is indexed.
- ANN-020 (annotation persistence API) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. Migration `0025` adds `annotations` (PostGIS geometry in level-0 pixels, SRID 0), the append-only `annotation_versions` and `slide_counters`, as [ADR 0076](adr/0076-annotation-and-ai-result-storage.md) set out. Eight routes under `/api/slides/{id}/annotations` create one or up to 10,000 shapes, list with a viewport and kind filter, get, edit and delete with optimistic versions, restore, and show the history. Kinds are regions of interest, measurements and person-made regions and cells, numbered R, M, T⁺ and C⁺ per slide without reuse. Only project owners draw until TASK-001 gives reviewers their task slides. 10,000 shapes are accepted in 1.27 s and listed in 0.2 s locally ([result](results/2026-10-18-annotation-bulk-latency.md)), and the test bound is 5 s because the API budgets are still TODO. The isolation and permission suites cover the new routes and tables. `Geometry(srid=0)` reflected as SRID -1 and broke the migration check, so the column has no SRID modifier and writes set SRID 0 themselves ([knowledge/database.md](knowledge/database.md)).
- STACK-021 (annotation and AI result storage model) done. [ADR 0076](adr/0076-annotation-and-ai-result-storage.md) keeps the backlog default: AI cells as gzip-served binary chunks (`PFC1`, a JSON header and aligned typed arrays) on a 2048 px grid with a summary in number order, AI regions and zones as immutable PostGIS rows, and what people draw in `annotations` with an append-only `annotation_versions`. Chunks carry type probabilities, which the POC format dropped. A [spike](results/2026-10-18-cell-chunk-paging-spike.md) converted the POC's 1.6 million cell job and paged it in Chromium: the overview was ready in 77 ms locally and 3.3 s at 100 ms and 50 Mbit/s, detail views took under 0.5 s on that link, and at most 65 MB was held, against the POC's 3.3 s local and 86 to 191 MB. Drawing was not measured and goes to the VIEW epic. ANN-020 (annotation persistence API) is now ready, and RSET-001 (result set model and API) waits only on it.
- Ticked INGEST-010 (thumbnail and overview image), INGEST-011 (label and macro image extraction) and INGEST-012 (tissue mask) after a green `ci` run on `main`. The `formats` job ran 100 ingest tests with 32 skips (the `big` and `camelyon` rows).
- INGEST-012 (tissue mask) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `parfocal_ingest.tissue` thresholds HSV saturation with Otsu (at least 20) on a grid of 64 px cells and grows the mask by one cell, stored as a 1-bit PNG. On CAMELYON16 it covers 99.30% (tumor_009) and 99.71% (test_001) of the annotated tumour and marks 0.64% and 1.23% of the glass ([result](results/2026-10-18-tissue-mask-camelyon.md)). Without the margin it covered under 98%. Golden masks for both slides are in `workers/ingest/tests/golden/tissue`. The CAMELYON rows skip in CI, which still runs the small-slide, empty-glass and stained-patch tests.
- INGEST-011 (label and macro image extraction) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `parfocal_ingest.associated` extracts the label as PNG and the macro as JPEG through the reader of record. A fixture test asserts which images are present and their sizes for 23 fixtures, including SVS, NDPI and SCN, and that each decodes and is not blank. Storing them apart under restricted access waits for storage and SEC-009 (slide label and macro image handling). No label or macro image is committed to the repository.
- Ticked INGEST-006 (pyramid and level validation) after a green `ci` run on `main`. The `formats` job ran 62 ingest tests with 16 skips (the `big` and `camelyon` rows).
- INGEST-010 (thumbnail and overview image) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0075](adr/0075-slide-thumbnails-and-overviews.md) renders a 1024 px overview and a 256 px thumbnail as JPEG from the slide pyramid through the reader of record, cropped to the region for Leica SCN and flattened onto the background colour. A golden test at 128 px covers the same 23 fixtures as the level table, the DICOM copy of an SVS must match the SVS, and a YCbCr-as-RGB misread fails the golden check by more than ten times the tolerance. Every golden was looked at before committing. Pillow is now a direct ingest dependency. The test helper `slide_support.opened` unpacks zips for both test files.
- INGEST-006 (pyramid and level validation) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `parfocal_ingest.pyramid` builds the level table (downsample, content size, stored size, tile size, compression and photometric interpretation) from `tifffile` series, OpenSlide for MIRAX and wsidicom for DICOM, which are now ingest dependencies. Factors are recovered with the tile-padding rule, the largest non-associated series is the slide, and the pixel size and objective power come from the reader of record with an estimate and a notice when one is missing. `Leica-1.scn` gives the 36832×38432 slide and not the 1616×4668 macro, `Leica-3.scn` is refused as multi-region and `Ventana-1.bif` as unreadable. A table test asserts the level table of 23 fixtures. The `big` and `camelyon` rows (OS-1.bif among them) skip in CI and passed locally with the whole corpus (78 tests). Two surprises: `tifffile` already reports Philips content sizes, and the DICOM copy of an Aperio JPEG 2000 file says `YBR_ICT` where the SVS says RGB. The rules are in [knowledge/slide-formats.md](knowledge/slide-formats.md).
- Ticked INGEST-003 (ingest workflow skeleton) and INGEST-004 (format identification) after green `ci` runs on `main`. The first `formats` job took 6 minutes to fetch the fixtures cold and ran 32 tests with 8 skips (the `big` group and `tumor_009.tif`).
- INGEST-004 (format identification) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `parfocal_ingest.identify` names the format and variant from magic bytes, `tifffile` series flags and the Software tag, and refuses CZI, ZVI, iSyntax, plain pictures, damaged TIFFs, zips without a slide and multi-file formats sent alone, each with a plain reason. A table test identifies every fixture of the small and formats groups (18 files, plus the two synthetic slides). A new `formats` CI job fetches those groups into the Actions cache and requires them, so the corpus check cannot silently skip. The `big` group skips until a machine has it. The rules are in [knowledge/slide-formats.md](knowledge/slide-formats.md). The identify step in the ingest workflow is still a placeholder until uploads reach storage.
- Ticked NOTIF-001 (notification service) after a green `ci` run on `main` that includes it.
- INGEST-003 (ingest workflow skeleton) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `parfocal_common.jobs` holds the job, step and unit tables (migration `0024`) and the claim, lease, renew and resume engine from ADR 0044, with claims under the platform scope (`platform_claims` policy) and steps under the workspace. `parfocal_ingest` has the seven-step ingest workflow with placeholder steps and a worker command, and `GET /api/projects/{id}/jobs/{job_id}` shows the status. A test kills a real worker process with SIGKILL mid-step, a second worker finishes the job, and every unit is written exactly once. It passed six runs in a row. How it works is in [knowledge/jobs.md](knowledge/jobs.md).
- Ticked SEC-024 (audit log queries and signed export) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- NOTIF-000 (research check) done and NOTIF-001 (notification service) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0074](adr/0074-notifications-as-rows-with-an-email-outbox.md) confirms STACK-036 (Resend terms rechecked, still no BAA, so email still carries no PHI) and stores notifications as rows (migration `0023`) routed by per-kind preferences with defaults. Emergency access is mandatory and cannot be turned off. Email is pending until `deliver_pending_emails` sends it after commit, and emergency access now notifies the project owners. `ApiAs` in the tests now exposes the app, so tests can read the memory email sender.
- Ticked GUIDE-006 (opinion form builder) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- SEC-024 (audit log queries and signed export) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0073](adr/0073-audit-queries-and-signed-export.md) adds filters by person, patient, case, project, action and dates to `listAuditEvents`, a signed `exportAuditEvents` document with emergency entries and their reasons marked, and `listAuditSigningKeys`. The Ed25519 key is derived per workspace from the master key with HKDF, so no new secret was needed. Tests verify the signature, refuse a tampered export and another workspace's key, and check every exported hash against the full chain. The isolation suite's member now has the seeded user's real subject, because the export writes an audit entry under that pseudonym.
- GUIDE-006 (opinion form builder) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`, as [ADR 0072](adr/0072-decisions-reasons-and-opinion-forms.md) set out. `opinion_forms` (migration `0022`, the first JSONB column) holds immutable versions per project. Every new project gets version 1 from the default template, and `PUT /api/projects/{id}/opinion-form` publishes the next version only when the questions changed. The questions are scale, choice, yes or no and text, with at most 12 per form and 3 quick ones. Old versions stay readable by number. Owners publish, members read, and each publish is audited. The question models live in `parfocal_api.opinion_questions`, so project creation can use them without an import cycle.
- GUIDE-000 (research check for decisions and the opinion form) done. [ADR 0072](adr/0072-decisions-reasons-and-opinion-forms.md) keeps one current decision per task, reviewer, slide and object, with every change in the audit log. It codes four Disagree reasons per object kind from the mock, merging its taxonomy-specific cell reasons into `wrong_class` with a chosen class, and makes the opinion form an immutable versioned list of scale, choice, yes or no and text questions, pinned by each task. Unsure zones, ROIs and measurements are not judged.
- Ticked FOUND-011 (structured logging) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- OBJ-000 (research check for object IDs) done. [ADR 0071](adr/0071-object-id-grammar.md) fixes the grammar from the kit's ID system page: T for AI regions of any class numbered by area, C for cells numbered once per slide in reading order (so T2·C145 and C145 are one cell), R, Z and M, a person mark (`⁺` on screen, `+` in ASCII), split suffixes and merge aliases. Full paths are percent-encoded per segment with `~` for a loose slide, which closes the ADR 0062 TODO. IDs carry across result sets one to one at IoU 0.5 for regions and 4 µm between centroids for cells. Both are project settings, because the kit lists them as an open question, and the 4 µm is an assumption until MLEVAL checks it.
- RSET-000 (research check for result sets and AI result import) done. [ADR 0070](adr/0070-result-file-parsers.md) reads Parfocal JSON and GeoJSON (including QuPath 0.7 exports) with typed msgspec decoding and shapely, CSV and TSV with the `csv` module, and masks and heatmaps with tifffile or Pillow and OpenCV traced per label in its bounding box, all into one object form in the ingest worker. A [spike](results/2026-10-18-result-parsers-spike.md) on Python 3.14 decoded 200,000 cells in 0.50 s and traced 3,000 mask labels in 0.037 s, and showed that tracing the binary mask merges touching objects. The draft schema is [parfocal-results-v1.schema.json](reference/parfocal-results-v1.schema.json). QuPath field details are second-hand until RSET-003 tests a real export.
- TASK-000 (research check for review tasks) done. [ADR 0069](adr/0069-slide-pipeline.md) derives every pipeline state from stored facts (the slide and its ingest status, result sets and jobs, assignments with `submitted_at`, sign-off records and exports) with one pure function, and keeps a table of the four moves people make: submit, reopen a review, sign off and reopen a task. No state machine library is added. When sign-off is allowed was moved to ADJ-000 (research check for disagreements and sign-off) for the owner to confirm.
- FOUND-011 (structured logging with tenant and request context) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0068](adr/0068-structured-logging-on-the-standard-library.md) builds JSON logs on the standard library in `parfocal_common.structured_logging`, with the ADR 0040 scrubber on the handler. The API middleware `RequestLogging` sets the request id (and returns `X-Request-Id`), the connection dependencies bind the workspace and pseudonymous user ids, and each request ends with one `request finished` line using the route template. Reading or adding a patient registers the name and MRN for redaction. Tests show a patient name and MRN passed to a logger come out as `[redacted]`, in a unit test, inside a request and after `read_patient`.
- SKEL-003 (minimal tenant, user, project and slide tables) ticked without new code. Its "Done when" was already met by PROJ-001 (project API) and ADAPT-004 (cases and slides in projects): `test_projects.py` creates a project in the user's workspace, `test_another_workspace_gets_nothing` gets 404 for it from another workspace, and CI on `main` is green.

## 2026-10-17

- Ticked PROJ-004 (class taxonomy per project) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- PROJ-004 (class taxonomy per project) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0067](adr/0067-class-taxonomy-and-colour-check.md) adds `project_classes` and `class_mappings` (migration `0021`), seven routes including the template list and a resolve route that RSET-005 will use, and a Python port of the POC colour check in `parfocal_api.colours` that matches the POC script to the last digits. Classes must be 15 apart in CIEDE2000 under normal vision and three simulated deficiencies, and clear of the reserved marks. PanNuke's Epithelial got #43999d, because the mock reused the amber of Dead ([result](results/2026-10-17-class-colour-separation.md)). The dependency on DS-002 was dropped, and DS-002 now points at the same limits.
- Consolidated memory after the ADAPT epic closed. "Where we are now" and "Next up" in `docs/README.md` were rewritten, M3 (review loop) is in progress in the roadmap, R13 (half-used hospital code) is closed and R14 (editing applied migrations) is new. The lessons on project access, the test helpers, the order of the permission matrix test, pinning a parent's mode with a composite key and the end of in-place migration edits went into `knowledge/api.md` and `knowledge/database.md`. ADR 0023, still Proposed, was marked superseded by ADR 0053, because the hospital rules it proposed are now ideas. Every ticked task of the epic has a log entry, and ADRs 0054 to 0066 are in the index.
- Ticked ADAPT-010 (emergency access for clinical projects) after a green `ci` run on `main`. The ADAPT epic is finished.
- ADAPT-010 (emergency access for clinical projects) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0066](adr/0066-emergency-access-per-clinical-project.md) supersedes ADR 0047. `POST /api/projects/{id}/emergency-access` opens one clinical project read-only for the workspace's emergency hours, for researchers and admins outside it. The database refuses a grant on a de-identified project, the audit entry is `project.emergency-access`, and outsiders without a grant get 403 `emergency-access-needed` on a clinical project and 404 on a de-identified one. `case_access.py` was folded into `project_access.py`. Notifying the owners waits for NOTIF-001.
- Ticked PROJ-003 (project roles) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- PROJ-003 (project roles) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0065](adr/0065-project-role-enforcement.md) puts the project matrix in `permissions.PROJECT_MATRIX`, combines it with the workspace grant in `permissions.in_project` and applies it to `getCase`, `listCases` and `createCase` through `parfocal_api.project_access`. A workspace reviewer who is a project viewer reads without changing. A project reviewer reads nothing until TASK-001 adds tasks. A project owner cannot become a workspace reviewer (409 `owns-projects`). At the owner's request, the rule that only workspace members reach workspace routes is now tested on every route. `test_project_roles.py` runs ten routes for seven role pairs from the matrix, and the permission page shows the project matrix.
- Ticked PROJ-001 (project model and API) after a green `ci` run on `main`.
- PROJ-001 (project model and API) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0064](adr/0064-project-api.md) sets the contract: projects are numbered per workspace under an advisory lock (P01, P02, never reused), a member lists and reads only the projects they are in, only an owner changes, archives, restores or manages members, and an archived project refuses changes and new cases. Migration `0020` adds the colour, template, `archived_at` and creator. `parfocal_api.projects` has seven routes, each change is audited, and `tests/test_projects.py`, the permission matrix and the isolation suite cover them. The taxonomy waits for PROJ-004, and case routes get the project role in PROJ-003.
- Ticked ADAPT-004 (cases for projects), ADAPT-005 (case creation) and ADAPT-011 (patients in clinical projects) after green `ci` runs on `main`. Only ADAPT-010 (emergency access for clinical projects) is left in the epic, and it waits for PROJ-001 (project model and API) and PROJ-003 (project roles).
- ADAPT-011 (patients only in clinical projects) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`, as [ADR 0062](adr/0062-project-model.md) set out. `patients` gained `project_id` and a `privacy_mode` column fixed to `clinical`, keyed to (`projects.id`, `projects.privacy_mode`), so the database refuses a patient in a de-identified project and refuses to make a project with patients de-identified. The MRN blind index and the birth date index are scoped to the project, and a case's patient key includes the project. `add_patient` and `find_patients` take the project. Tests cover each refusal in the database and through `POST /api/cases`, and the data map was regenerated. Migration `0012` was edited in place again.
- Ticked IAM-022 (researcher, reviewer and admin roles) after a green `ci` run on `main`. ADAPT-011 (patients only in clinical projects) now depends on ADAPT-004 alone, because the privacy mode column it needs exists, while PROJ-002 (privacy modes) waits on SEC-009 (label and macro images).
- ADAPT-005 (rework case creation) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `POST /api/cases` needs only `project_id` and `label`, and the patient and specimens are optional. Drafts, the service, `CaseDefaults` and the `case_creation.RULES` hook are gone, with the `service` and `is_draft` columns edited out of migration `0015`. API tests cover creation in each privacy mode, a case with only a project and an ID, idempotency and concurrent repeats. CASES-013, CASES-022 and CASES-023 still name the rule hook in the backlog as history and ideas.
- ADAPT-004 (rework cases for projects) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0063](adr/0063-cases-and-slides-in-projects.md) supersedes ADR 0036. Migration `0012`, renamed `0012_projects_and_cases.py`, now creates `projects` and `project_members` ahead of `cases`. Cases and slides belong to a project, a case is optional for a slide, case and slide labels are unique within their scope, and the hospital case columns are gone. `POST /api/cases` takes a project and a label and refuses patient data in a de-identified project. Local databases need `docker compose down -v` again.
- PROJ-000 (research check for projects) done. [ADR 0062](adr/0062-project-model.md), accepted by the owner, sets the project model after a look at Cytomine and OMERO: opening a project needs a project role for researchers too, project access is filtered in the API while row-level security stays per workspace, and each patient belongs to one clinical project, which a key on the project's privacy mode enforces in the database. The ADR lists what `cases`, `slides`, `patients`, `emergency_grants` and the new `projects`, `project_members` and `result_sets` tables become. ADAPT-004, ADAPT-010, ADAPT-011 and PROJ-003 point at it now.
- IAM-022 (researcher, reviewer and admin roles with project roles) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0061](adr/0061-researcher-reviewer-and-admin-roles.md) supersedes ADR 0026. The five hospital roles became researcher, reviewer and admin, `ProjectRole` names owner, reviewer and viewer for PROJ-003, five permissions and the service account scopes were renamed, and the role checks in migrations `0005` and `0008` were edited in place. The isolation suite now picks the role per GET route, because no single role reads them all. A draft case is now seen only by its author. The e2e invitation test passed locally against Zitadel.
- Ticked ADAPT-002 (folders) and ADAPT-001 (hospital-only permissions and the guest scope) after green `ci` runs on `main`. Every pure revert of [ADR 0054](adr/0054-hospital-code-inventory-and-revert-order.md) is done. The rest of the ADAPT epic waits on PROJ-000 (project model) and PROJ-002 and PROJ-003 (privacy modes and project roles).
- ADAPT-001 (remove hospital-only permissions, grants and the guest scope) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0060](adr/0060-no-sign-out-permissions-or-guest-scope.md) removes 13 permissions, four grants and the outside consultant column, so the matrix has 13 permissions and five roles. The guest principal, the case and view settings in `DatabaseScope` and the two guest policies are gone, and migrations `0006`, `0007` and `0012` were edited in place. Nothing had ever created a guest principal.
- Ticked ADAPT-008 (routing), ADAPT-009 (availability), ADAPT-003 (signing rights), ADAPT-007 (case state machine) and ADAPT-006 (accession numbers) after green `ci` runs on `main`.
- ADAPT-002 (revert folders) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0059](adr/0059-no-folders.md) supersedes ADR 0046. Folders, folder members, `all_folders`, the folder on a case, the six folder endpoints and the "in their folders" grant are gone, and migrations `0016` and `0017` were deleted. Until PROJ-003 (project roles) every member sees what their role allows across the workspace. The emergency check moved to `parfocal_api.case_access`, and `open-outside-folders` became `emergency-access`, held only by administrators.
- ADAPT-006 (revert accession numbers) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0058](adr/0058-typed-case-ids.md) supersedes ADR 0037. The templates, counters, their endpoints and audit action are gone, and migration `0013` was deleted, so `0015` now follows `0012`. `POST /api/cases` requires a typed `accession` that is unique within the workspace and answers 409 `accession-taken` otherwise. Every test that creates a case now sends one.
- ADAPT-007 (revert the case state machine) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0057](adr/0057-no-case-state-machine.md) supersedes ADR 0038. `parfocal_api.case_states`, cancel and restore, the state columns, the state filter and three audit actions are gone. Migration `0014` was deleted and the `state` column left `0012`. The `hypothesis` dev dependency went too, because only the state machine tests used it.
- ADAPT-003 (revert signing rights and supervision) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0056](adr/0056-no-signing-rights.md) supersedes ADR 0032. `parfocal_api.signing`, its four endpoints, the signing and signature columns and their audit actions are gone. Migration `0010` was deleted and `0011` now follows `0009`, so local databases need `docker compose down -v` before `pnpm db:migrate`. The co-sign grants stay in the matrix until ADAPT-001 (hospital-only permissions).
- ADAPT-009 (rework availability) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0055](adr/0055-away-until-with-a-note.md) supersedes ADR 0051: a membership is available or away, away may have an end and a note of up to 200 characters, and busy, the back-up and the rerouting of new cases are gone. Migration `0019` was edited in place, so local databases need `docker compose down -v` before `pnpm db:migrate`. The workspace and person helpers moved from `test_folder_access.py` to `tests/workspace_support.py`, so later tests can make a workspace without folders.
- ADAPT-008 (revert case routing rules) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `parfocal_api.routing` and `test_routing.py` are deleted. Nothing called them, so no endpoint or schema changed. CASES-011 (case assignment and routing) stays ticked as history, and researchers assign reviewers in a task (TASK-001) instead.
- ADAPT-000 (inventory and plan for the built hospital code) done. [ADR 0054](adr/0054-hospital-code-inventory-and-revert-order.md), accepted by the owner, lists every hospital endpoint, table, column, permission, grant and helper as kept, reworked or reverted, with the task that does it. The order is pure reverts first (ADAPT-008 routing, ADAPT-009 availability, ADAPT-003 signing, ADAPT-007 case states, ADAPT-006 accession numbers, ADAPT-002 folders), then ADAPT-001 (hospital-only permissions, grants and the guest scope), then IAM-022 (the new roles), then cases into projects. The owner said the code is throwaway, so migrations `0010` to `0019` are edited in place and nothing is migrated. Surprises: nothing ever creates a guest principal, so the guest scope goes with ADAPT-001, and the role check never let an outside consultant hold a membership, so there was nothing to remove for them. ADAPT-001 was narrowed and the role swap moved to IAM-022, and ADAPT-004 now creates the minimal `projects` table so that it and PROJ-001 (project model) no longer wait on each other.
- Re-planned the backlog for the AI results review direction at the owner's request ([ADR 0053](adr/0053-ai-results-review-direction.md), accepted on the owner's decisions in POC [doc 37](https://github.com/fedasevich/pathlogy-poc/blob/master/docs/37-ai-results-review-rework.md) to [doc 41](https://github.com/fedasevich/pathlogy-poc/blob/master/docs/41-clickable-pass.md)). Thirteen epics were added: PROJ (projects, privacy modes, taxonomy and templates), RSET (result sets and AI result import), OBJ (object IDs), TASK (review tasks and the slide pipeline), GUIDE (decisions, guided review and the opinion form), ADJ (disagreements, adjudication and sign-off), EVAL (evaluation report and exports), JOBS (model runs, the jobs tray and lab models), REMOTE (remote sources, webhooks and events), ONB (sign-up, setup and the sample project), ADMIN (admin page), VIZ (layers and visualization) and ADAPT (reverting or reworking the built hospital code). IAM-022 (researcher, reviewer and admin roles), DS-018 to DS-022 (surfaces, progress, token field, undo toast, modals), CASE-014 and CASE-015 (inspector tabs, progress ring) and WL-018 and WL-019 (Assigned by me, All slides) were added. WL became My work and CASE the slide view, and tasks across SKEL, IAM, SEC, INGEST, DS, SHELL, HOME, CASE, REVIEW, ANN, COLLAB, CASES, AIP, NOTIF, SET, CMD and PILOT were edited to the new model. REPORT, BOARD, TEAM and TB have the milestone Idea, and single hospital tasks start with `Idea:`, 91 in all, with no planned task depending on one. M1 now ends with a decision and a submitted review, and M3 to M5 were redefined. The owner asked mid-way for tasks that revert or rework the built hospital code, which became the ADAPT epic, one task per module. The CASES-025 (folder screens) claim was released because folders are an idea. `board.py check`, the ADR check and the docs unit tests pass.

## 2026-10-15

- FOUND-020 (task board over the backlog) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `pnpm board` serves a kanban page from `scripts/board.py` and `scripts/board.html` that re-reads the backlog every 4 seconds, with columns for waiting, ready, in progress, review, blocked and done, a dependency tree both ways per task, and links from each task to the ADRs, results, docs and log lines that name it. Work in progress lives in one claim file per task under [board/active/](board/README.md), so parallel agents do not conflict, and the CLI gives agents `ready`, `show`, `tree`, `claim`, `move`, `release` and `check` ([ADR 0052](adr/0052-task-board-from-backlog-and-claim-files.md), Proposed). 13 tests in `scripts/test_board.py` cover parsing, statuses, claims, trees, the check and the static page, and the docs workflow runs `board.py check`. The blocked tasks from "Next up" were seeded as claim files held by the owner. Surprises: only 122 of 606 tasks have a "Depends on" line, so the ready column is wide, and the docs workflow had been red on `main` since the STACK-030 (deploy pipeline and previews) commit because ADR 0003's status line held an amendment note. The note now has its own `Amended by` line.
- IAM-015 (availability status and back-up) done after CI run 38697750929 passed on `main`. Stopped for now at the owner's request, with nothing in progress.
- STACK-026 (slide reading on the server) done, run by a parallel agent on Sonnet and reviewed before commit. [ADR 0043](adr/0043-server-slide-reading.md), accepted under the owner's standing approval, makes OpenSlide 4.0.1 the reader of record for every format it opens (MRXS and stitched Ventana BIF included), tiffslide the reader for OME-TIFF and the second reader in tests, tifffile the validator of every upload and wsidicom the DICOM reader. Files no reader accepts are refused, not converted, and a pixel size above 100 microns or missing is refused. Results: [results/2026-10-15-server-slide-reading-spike.md](results/2026-10-15-server-slide-reading-spike.md). Surprises: `CMU-1.tiff` reports 1000 microns per pixel, `Ventana-1.bif` fails in OpenSlide while `OS-1.bif` opens, and tiffslide differs from OpenSlide by about 0.44 on JPEG 2000 YCbCr slides. Open: OME-Zarr and real OME-TIFF fixtures, range reads from R2, TIAToolbox, and whether LGPL `openslide-bin` is fine for any on-premise offer (owner).
- STACK-003 (routing) done, run by a parallel agent on Sonnet and reviewed before commit. [ADR 0048](adr/0048-routing.md), accepted under the owner's standing approval, picks TanStack Router 1.170 with file-based routes, Zod 4 search params and automatic code splitting. A spike outside the repository built `/case/:caseId/slide/:slideId?x=&y=&z=` with TanStack Router, React Router 7.18 and 8.4. TanStack's links rejected a wrong type, a missing param and an unknown search key, while React Router's plain string links passed typecheck. Main chunks differed by about 3 KB gzip. Results: [results/2026-10-15-routing-spike.md](results/2026-10-15-routing-spike.md). Surprises: without `addExtensions: true` under `nodenext` the route tree silently types as `any`, and React Compiler 1.0 skipped a component that destructures with default values. Nothing ran in a browser yet.
- STACK-004 (server state and data fetching) done, run by a parallel agent on Sonnet and reviewed before commit. [ADR 0049](adr/0049-server-state-and-data-fetching.md), accepted under the owner's standing approval, picks TanStack Query v5 through the generated Orval hooks, with no cache persister because cached data holds PHI, a cache cleared on sign-out, lock and workspace switch, realtime events that only invalidate, optimistic annotation edits with rollback and up to 3 retries on network or server errors, and Suspense per route or panel. The spike passed 16 of 16 tests. Results: [results/2026-10-15-server-state-spike.md](results/2026-10-15-server-state-spike.md). Surprise: the generated keys are flat URL strings, so invalidating the case list does not touch a case, and the generated hooks type errors as `Problem` while the fetcher throws `ApiError`, a follow-up for STACK-008 (generated client).
- STACK-030 (deploy pipeline and previews) done as a paper decision by a parallel agent on Sonnet, reviewed before commit, with no cloud calls. [ADR 0050](adr/0050-deploy-pipeline-and-previews.md), accepted under the owner's standing approval, keeps Kubernetes out, gives each pull request its own edge Worker serving the web app, a Modal app in a `preview` environment and a Neon branch with a 7-day expiry in a separate preview project, at most 5 at once, with secrets generated per pull request and none for forks. Teardown runs on close, in a nightly sweep and through vendor expiry. It amends ADR 0003, because Cloudflare gives no version URLs to Workers with Durable Objects, so the web app is served as the Worker's assets instead of a Pages project. OPS-001, OPS-002, OPS-003 and OPS-005 (base infrastructure, deploy definitions, environments and continuous deployment) now point to it. Surprise: Neon's plans page now says 1 GB per project on Free while ADR 0004 says 0.5 GB.
- IAM-015 (availability status and back-up) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0051](adr/0051-availability-status-and-back-up.md), accepted under the owner's standing approval, stores the status on the membership (migration `0019`): busy until a time of at most 24 hours, or away with a back-up who is another member and not away. A status past its end reads as available. A new case assigned to an away person goes to the back-up, and the routing rules prefer the back-up of an away person. `GET`/`PUT /api/me/availability` and `GET /api/tenant/availability` serve it. 13 tests in `test_availability.py` and 2 new tests in `test_routing.py` cover each status, its expiry, invalid statuses and back-ups, the team list and a new case going to the back-up. Mutation checks removed the expiry, the hand-over to the back-up and the routing preference, and each was caught.
- IAM-017 (emergency access with a reason) done after CI run 38639946496 passed on `main`, and CASES-011 (case assignment and routing) done after CI run 38640181715 passed.
- STACK-031 (model licensing policy) put on hold. The owner said Parfocal will not really be commercial and licensing does not matter for now, so [ADR 0042](adr/0042-model-licensing-policy.md) was rejected and risk R1 now points to it for later.

## 2026-10-14

- Stopped for the day at the owner's request because of usage limits. Four parallel agents were stopped before finishing and left nothing in the repository: STACK-026 (server slide reading), STACK-003 (routing), STACK-004 (server state) and STACK-030 (deploy pipeline and previews). Their reserved ADR numbers 0043, 0048, 0049 and 0050 are free again.
- CASES-011 (case assignment and routing) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `parfocal_api.routing` holds the rules as pure functions: a case goes to an available person who works in its folder (or every folder) and reads its specimen types, the lowest share of capacity wins, nobody over capacity is chosen except for STAT, and the queue routes STAT first, then ASAP, urgent and routine, oldest first. 9 tests in `test_routing.py` cover each rule and a Hypothesis property over random queues. CASES-022 (assignment rules with a test run) will feed it real people, load and absences.
- IAM-016 (roles narrowed by folders) done after CI run 38639124771 passed on `main`.
- IAM-017 (emergency access with a reason) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0047](adr/0047-emergency-access-with-a-reason.md), accepted under the owner's standing approval, adds `POST /api/cases/{id}/emergency-access`, which takes a reason of 10 to 500 characters and opens that one case for the workspace's `emergency_hours` (default 4, set with the session policy, migration `0018`). Reading a case now passes inside the folders, for leads, or with an unexpired grant, and administrators reach patient data only this way (`emergency-access-needed` otherwise). The audit entry carries the reason. 10 tests in `test_emergency_access.py` cover a grant for one case, the audit reason, expiry, missing and too short reasons, an administrator reading a patient only with a reason, who may ask, and the lab-set duration. The folder lead's notification waits for NOTIF-001 (notification service). A mutation check that ignored the expiry was caught.
- STACK-022 (background jobs and durable workflows) done, run by a parallel agent on Sonnet and reviewed before commit. A spike on the local Postgres 17 behind PgBouncer ran a three-step workflow (scan, a 20-tile derive, publish), killed the worker with SIGKILL in each step, and a second worker resumed from the last completed tile or step with 22 distinct outputs and no duplicates, claiming the expired lease 5.1 to 6.1 s after the kill with a 6 s lease. Three workers over 12 jobs gave one claim per job. DBOS Transact 3.2.0 also resumed within 0.3 s but reran the interrupted tile, and a second live process triggered recovery of a running workflow. [ADR 0044](adr/0044-background-jobs-and-workflows.md), accepted under the owner's standing approval, keeps ADR 0003's Postgres tables with leases, with DBOS as the first candidate if the table code outgrows its job. Results: [results/2026-10-14-durable-workflow-spike.md](results/2026-10-14-durable-workflow-spike.md). Still TODO: the Modal spawn and cron resumer, a run against Neon's pooler and the row-level security path of the claim query.
- STACK-031 (model licensing policy) researched by a parallel agent on Sonnet, not ticked. The backlog marks it an open decision for the owner, so [ADR 0042](adr/0042-model-licensing-policy.md) is Proposed. It treats Parfocal as commercial from the first pilot and holds a licence matrix of 25 models and 10 datasets read from primary pages on 2026-10-14. Only SAM ViT, SAM 2 and H-optimus-0 would be allowed today. HoVer-NeXt, VISTA-2D, CellViT, CellViT++, NuLite and the non-commercial foundation models are blocked, and others stay closed until licence TODOs are filled. If accepted, no nucleus detector or typer is usable yet, so risk R1 stays open. Surprise: CAMELYON16 is CC0 on one challenge page and CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 on another.
- STACK-009 (forms and validation) done, written by a parallel agent on Sonnet and reviewed before commit. [ADR 0045](adr/0045-forms-and-validation.md), accepted under the owner's standing approval, picks TanStack Form 1.x with Zod 4 behind a Standard Schema boundary, and drives report forms from template data: a condition tree decides which fields are visible, one pure function validates only visible fields, only visible fields go to the API, and the server re-checks the rules. A spike outside the repository ([results/2026-10-14-forms-spike.md](results/2026-10-14-forms-spike.md)) compiled all four form approaches under TypeScript 7.0.2 strict and React Compiler 1.0.0 and measured bundle sizes. Open: a browser render check, Orval's Zod output and a bundle budget.
- IAM-016 (roles narrowed by folders) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0046](adr/0046-roles-narrowed-by-folders.md), accepted under the owner's standing approval, applies the ten folder-scoped permissions only inside a person's folders, keeps unfiled cases visible to everyone who holds the permission, and lets only leads work in every folder (migration `0017` adds `memberships.all_folders` with a check constraint). Opening a case outside the folders answers 403 `outside-folders`, creating or filing a case outside them is refused, and the new `GET /api/cases` (`listCases`) hides cases of other folders. The permission test now runs every permission for every role inside and outside the folders, and 4 tests in `test_folder_access.py` cover the list, opening, creating and filing, and the every-folder switch. A mutation check removed the folder check on opening a case and was caught. Surprise: the CASES-003 (folders) tests filed cases as a lead and as lab staff outside their folders, which the new rule refuses, so their setup now gives the lead every folder.
- CASES-003 (folders by service) done after CI run 38637884711 passed on `main`. CI run 38638072260 also passed with STACK-032 (observability and the PHI scrubber) at the tip.
- SEC-000 (compliance control matrix) done, written by a parallel agent on Sonnet and reviewed before commit. [compliance/controls.md](compliance/controls.md) maps 31 GDPR articles, 61 HIPAA Security Rule standards and specifications and all 93 ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Annex A controls to backlog tasks, to policy work, or to a gap. The 9 gaps are GDPR's privacy notice, restriction of processing, telling recipients of changes and the right to object, HIPAA log-in monitoring, and ISO threat intelligence, data leakage prevention, clock synchronization and network segregation. Physical controls are policy because the cloud providers supply them. Surprise: the agent did not use web search, so the control names come from memory and are marked as needing a check against the standards. The HIPAA documentation retention period is a TODO for counsel.
- IAM-000 (identity and authorization research check) done, written by a parallel agent on Sonnet and reviewed before commit. [ADR 0041](adr/0041-identity-and-authorization-rechecked.md) confirms [ADR 0020](adr/0020-authorization-rls-scopes-and-rbac.md) (row-level security with roles in code) and [ADR 0025](adr/0025-zitadel-bff-login.md) (Zitadel behind our backend) without change against October 2026 sources. Risks it records: Zitadel's free plan states no uptime guarantee, Zitadel had ten advisories (two critical) from July to September 2026 without saying whether its cloud was affected, Login V2 changes may break the logout E2E test, ID tokens carry email and profile only when an instance setting is on, which IAM-001 (Zitadel set up from code) must keep, and the free plan allows only three identity providers, which may limit IAM-012 (per-organisation SSO). Some facts came from page summaries and are marked TODO.
- STACK-032 (observability) done, written by a parallel agent on Sonnet and reviewed before commit. [ADR 0040](adr/0040-observability-and-phi-scrubbing.md), accepted under the owner's standing approval, keeps OpenTelemetry to the Grafana Cloud free tier and the Sentry free plan in its EU region, exporting only from production, and defines one PHI scrubbing rule set: an allow-list of structured fields holding only opaque ids, deny by data class from ADR 0028, redaction of registered sensitive strings, emails and bearer tokens in free text, and a Sentry `before_send` that drops request data and keeps only the pseudonymous user id. `parfocal_common.scrubbing` implements it, and `test_scrubbing.py` shows a made-up patient name redacted from a log line, an exception and a Sentry event. Open TODOs: the free-plan limits came from third-party pages and need checking against the vendors, Grafana's EU region name, and the TypeScript scrubber for the web app and edge.
- STACK-036 (email provider) done, written by a parallel agent on Sonnet and reviewed before commit. [ADR 0039](adr/0039-email-through-resend-with-phi-rules.md), accepted under the owner's standing approval, keeps Resend's HTTP API in production only behind the `EmailSender` interface. Resend stores data in the US and cannot sign a BAA, so email never carries PHI: fixed subjects, bodies that only say something happened, and links with opaque tokens or ids. Bounces and complaints arrive on a Svix-signed webhook, are deduplicated, feed a suppression list and mark the address undeliverable, and a bounced invitation becomes undeliverable for the admin to fix. Amazon SES is the named fallback with its triggers. Open TODOs: SES storage per region, the Postmark and SendGrid BAA and webhook facts, pilot email volume, and the owner confirming Resend as a US subprocessor.
- CASES-003 (folders by service) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. It was split: the folder screens and their component tests moved to the new CASES-025, because they need the final UI. Migration `0016` adds `folders` (name unique per workspace, one of the 12 colours, archiving) and `folder_members`, whose people must be members of the workspace, and gives each case an optional folder through a key that includes the workspace. Members list folders with their open case count and people, leads create, rename, recolour and archive them ("lab setup"), leads and administrators choose their people ("manage people"), and anyone who may edit cases files a case with `PUT /api/cases/{id}/folder`. Case creation now takes a folder too. Every change is audited. 6 tests in `test_folders.py` cover counts that leave out drafts and signed cases, people, moving a case with its audit entry, archived folders, unique names, non-members, another workspace's folder and who may change folders. A mutation check removed the archived-folder rule and was caught. Surprise: case creation with another workspace's folder hit the tenant-bound foreign key with a 500, so it now checks the folder first and answers 404.
- CASES-013 (case creation API) done after CI run 38636848225 passed on `main`.
- CASES-013 (case creation API) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `POST /api/cases` (`createCase`, needs "create a case") takes an existing patient or a new one, specimens with blocks and their expected stains and levels, clinical note and question, requester, priority and optional service, due time, assignee and accession. Migration `0015` adds those case and block columns and `case_requests` for idempotency keys. The number comes from CASES-004 (accession number rules), or an accession from a requisition is checked against the format. Defaults come from a `RULES` hook that fills nothing until the rule tasks CASES-022 (assignment), CASES-023 (due time) and AIP-016 (AI plan) arrive. Drafts are seen only by their author and lab staff. A repeated `Idempotency-Key` from the same person returns the same case, serialised by an advisory lock. 15 tests in `test_case_creation.py` cover creation with a new and an existing patient, rule defaults and overrides, a non-member assignee, draft visibility, repeats, five concurrent repeats, requisition accessions and invalid drafts. Mutation checks removed the lock and the draft rule, and each was caught. Surprise: a new patient whose MRN already existed caused a 500, so it now answers 409 `patient-exists`.
- CASES-012 (case state machine) done after CI run 38635743160 passed on `main`.
- CASES-012 (case state machine) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0038](adr/0038-case-state-machine.md), accepted under the owner's standing approval, models the kit's ten states as 14 named events, each with its starting states, its result and who may cause it. `move_case` applies an event with a row lock and an audit entry, and leads cancel and restore cases through `POST /api/cases/{id}/cancel` and `/restore` (migration `0014` keeps the state before cancelling). Tests in `test_case_states.py` check all 700 combinations of state, event and actor against a table written from the kit, prove every state reachable, use Hypothesis walks over allowed moves to show that "signed" is reached only by signing and that a signed case is never cancelled, and cover cancel, restore, the 30-day limit and who may cancel. Hypothesis 6.168 joined the dev dependencies, pinned below the release published today. Mutation checks added a shortcut from review to signed, removed the restore limit, allowed cancelling a signed case and dropped co-signing from the signing events, and each was caught. Surprise: the first Hypothesis walks picked random moves and almost never reached "signed", so they missed a mutation the table caught. The kit lets lab staff cancel cases that wait for slides while the matrix does not, which ADR 0038 leaves to the owner.
- CASES-004 (accession number rules) done after CI run 38634802970 passed on `main`.
- FOUND-013 (local Postgres stack) fixed. A CI run failed its migration with "the database system is starting up", because the compose health check used the Unix socket, which the image's temporary first-start server also answers. The check now connects over TCP to `127.0.0.1`. A fresh local volume came up and migrated with the change.
- CASES-004 (accession number rules) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0037](adr/0037-accession-number-templates.md), accepted under the owner's standing approval, gives each workspace a template such as the kit's `{prefix}{yy}-{seq:5}` (`S26-04830`) and a counter per prefix and year (migration `0013`). `allocate_accession` hands out numbers inside the case's transaction and skips numbers the LIS already used, `check_imported_accession` validates numbers from imports, and `/api/tenant/accession-format` shows and changes the format with a preview of the next numbers. 17 tests in `test_accessions.py` cover rendering and matching, ten bad templates, counting per prefix, year and workspace, 25 concurrent allocations, skipping imported numbers, imported numbers that are malformed or taken, a full range and the format change with its audit entry. Mutation checks removed the skip and loosened the digit count, and each was caught. The first CI run failed on a weak SEC-004 (encrypted patient identifiers) test: it checked that `123` did not appear in the stored row, and random ciphertext in hex happened to contain it. The test now looks only for plaintext that cannot occur in hex output.
- CASES-001 (patient and case data model) done after CI run 38633250128 passed on `main`.
- CASES-001 (patient and case data model) implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. Migration `0012` adds sex and age to `patients` and creates `cases`, `specimens`, `blocks` and `slides` as [ADR 0036](adr/0036-case-data-model.md) describes. Every child row's foreign key includes `tenant_id`, because foreign key checks ignore row-level security and a plain key would let one workspace point at another workspace's case. Guests see only their shared case and its specimens, blocks and slides, and no patient rows, through new restrictive policies (`enable_guest_case_scope`, `hide_from_guests`). Clinical notes and questions are encrypted like patient identifiers. `GET /api/cases/{id}` (`getCase`, needs "see cases") returns the case with its patient, specimens, blocks and slides, without the patient and clinical text for guests. 10 tests in `test_cases.py` cover the full read, ciphertext at rest, the guest view, the guest scope in the database, cross-workspace references, a block filed under another case's specimen, refusals and invalid codes, and the permission and isolation suites now seed a real encrypted case. Mutation checks let guests see patients and dropped the workspace from the specimen key, and each was caught. Surprise: a plain foreign key is a cross-tenant hole even with forced row-level security. One local E2E run failed once when the login test waited more than 30 s for Zitadel's sign-in page, and the next run passed.
- CASES-000 (research check for cases) done. [ADR 0036](adr/0036-case-data-model.md), accepted under the owner's standing approval, sets the model from the UX kit's "Case lifecycle and data" page: patient, case, specimen, block and slide, the kit's ten states, FHIR's codes for priority and sex, encrypted clinical text, and slides that belong to a case and optionally a block. The case's folder moved to CASES-003 (folders).
- STACK-040 (FHIR and HL7 libraries) done. A spike with fhir.resources 8.3.0 on Python 3.14 built and parsed Patient, ServiceRequest, Specimen and DiagnosticReport in R4B and R5 at about 60 µs per patient round trip ([results/2026-10-14-fhir-resources-spike.md](results/2026-10-14-fhir-resources-spike.md)). [ADR 0035](adr/0035-fhir-r4-through-fhir-resources-r4b.md), accepted under the owner's standing approval, picks R4 on the wire through the R4B models, our own checks on codes and no FHIR server. Surprises: the library has no plain R4 package, and it accepts codes outside FHIR's value sets.
- At the owner's request, AGENTS.md gained a rule: a backlog ID is always followed by a few plain words saying what the task is.
- SEC-004 done after CI run 38624247570 passed on `main`.
- SEC-004 implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0034](adr/0034-blind-indexes-for-encrypted-patient-identifiers.md), accepted under the owner's standing approval, chooses whole-word blind indexes over trigrams. Migration `0011` adds `tenant_keys` (wrapped data and index keys, which only the platform scope may rewrap or delete) and a `patients` table holding only the encrypted name, MRN and birth date and their blind indexes. `parfocal_api.identifiers` creates, reads and searches patients, rotates data keys, rewraps under a new master key and shreds a workspace's keys. The API now needs `MASTER_KEYS`, which `.env.example`, the tests and the `e2e` CI job provide. 8 tests in `test_identifiers.py` cover ciphertext at rest, normalised search by name, MRN and birth date, other workspaces finding nothing, one MRN per workspace, a value moved to another patient failing to decrypt, shredding, data-key rotation and master-key rotation. Mutation checks let a workspace shred its own keys, used one index key for all workspaces and dropped the patient id from the associated data, and each was caught. Surprises: the first MRN normalisation kept hyphens, and the first rewrap test rewrapped every workspace's keys in the shared test database.
- STACK-037 done. [ADR 0033](adr/0033-envelope-encryption-with-tenant-data-keys.md), accepted under the owner's standing approval, keeps ADR 0003's application-level envelope encryption and defines master-key rotation (rewrap only the wrapped data keys), data-key rotation (versions in the field header) and crypto-shredding (delete a tenant's wrapped keys). `parfocal_common.envelope` implements it, with 13 tests, and a field costs under 2 µs to encrypt or decrypt on an M4, see [results/2026-10-14-envelope-encryption-spike.md](results/2026-10-14-envelope-encryption-spike.md). SEC-004 was edited to store wrapped keys in a `tenant_keys` table and to destroy its blind-index key with them. Surprise: unwrapping a data key costs the same as decrypting a field, so a key cache saves only the database read.
- Consolidated the docs, the first time since 2026-10-06 (43 log entries). "Where we are now" and "Next up" in the README were rewritten, the roadmap moved M1 to In progress, and the risk register gained R11 (paused cloud access blocks M0 and M1 gates) and R12 (ADRs accepted faster than they are reviewed). Every ticked task has a log entry. ADR 0023 is still Proposed while later ADRs build on it, which Next up asks the owner to settle. Two TODOs in accepted ADRs are now resolved: ADR 0024's question of how operators are recognised was answered by IAM-002's `platform_operators` table, and ADR 0026's unaudited role changes by SEC-003. Lessons from this period were already filed in `knowledge/` as they came up (auth, database, API).
- IAM-018 done after CI run 38622458693 passed on `main`.
- IAM-018 implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0032](adr/0032-signing-rights-on-the-membership.md), accepted under the owner's standing approval, puts signing rights on the membership (migration `0010`): a nullable "signs alone" override over the role's default, a supervisor, and a PNG signature on the person. `parfocal_api.signing.decide` is the one rule the sign-out tasks will call. Leads change rights through `PUT /api/tenant/members/{id}/signing`, administrators may not although the matrix lets them change roles, and the permission test lists that narrowing explicitly. People upload and remove their signature under `/api/me/signature`. 20 tests in `test_signing.py` cover 14 combinations with their refusal messages, the resident path to send-for-review and signing alone, the signature upload and its validation, invalid supervisors, who may change what, audit entries naming people only by pseudonym, and a role change resetting the override. Mutation checks removed the lead-only rule and the supervisor's own signing check, and each was caught. The kit's W48 notes were read from the UX kit artifact.
- IAM-009 done after CI run 38621711396 passed on `main`.
- IAM-009 implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. It was split: the lock screen and the return to the same camera position moved to the new IAM-021, because they need the final UI, as FOUND-019 was split from FOUND-012. [ADR 0031](adr/0031-idle-lock-from-explicit-activity.md), accepted under the owner's standing approval, bases the idle lock on explicit activity rather than any request, unlocks by signing in again with `prompt=login`, and keeps the limits in a new tenant-owned `session_policies` table (migration `0009`), which leads change. Sessions gained a public id, `active_at` and the user agent. `listSessions`, `endSession`, `endOtherSessions` and `recordActivity` live under `/api/auth`, and `getSessionPolicy` and `updateSessionPolicy` under `/api/tenant/session-policy`. 11 tests in `test_sessions.py` cover the lock, activity, background calls not counting, the workspace's idle time and lifetime, unlocking, the session list, ending others and one session, strangers' sessions and the policy. Mutation checks removed the idle check and the `prompt=login` and let background calls count, and each was caught. Surprise: the first version kept the policy on `tenants`, and the update returned 200 while row-level security silently skipped the row, which the test caught.
- IAM-007 done after CI run 38620733700 passed on `main`, including the two new invitation tests in the `e2e` job.
- IAM-007 implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0030](adr/0030-invitations-bound-to-verified-email.md), accepted under the owner's standing approval, binds an invitation to a verified email. Migration `0008` adds tenant-owned `invitations`. Leads and administrators invite, list and withdraw under `/api/tenant/invitations`, operators invite a workspace's first administrator under `/api/platform/tenants/{id}/invitations`, and `POST /api/invitations/accept` joins the signed-in person with the invited role and makes the workspace active. Links expire after 7 days, and inviting the same address again withdraws the earlier link. Every step is audited, including an expired link being used. Login now stores the email only when the IdP marks it verified. A new `file` email backend writes an outbox for local use and E2E. 13 API tests in `test_invitations.py`, two new Playwright tests in `invitations.spec.ts` against real Zitadel (invite, accept and expiry) and the permission and isolation suites cover it. Mutation checks removed the email match, the expiry check and the verified-email rule, and each was caught. Surprise: `psql -tAc` prints `INSERT 0 1` after the returned id.
- SEC-003, SEC-001 and IAM-011 done after CI run 38619381096 passed on `main`, including the `e2e` job. They were committed separately as `ebe23f9`, `fda49b2` and `18e2db2`.
- IAM-011 implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0029](adr/0029-service-accounts-with-own-api-tokens.md), accepted under the owner's standing approval, chooses our own hashed tokens over Zitadel machine users. Migration `0007` adds tenant-owned `service_accounts` and `api_tokens` and the `service` actor kind. `Authorization: Bearer pf_...` becomes a `ServicePrincipal` limited to its scopes (`see-cases`, `edit-cases`, `upload-slides`, `see-team`), and `/api/tenant/service-accounts` creates, lists, rotates, revokes and disables, with every step audited. `require()` gained `write=True`, which refuses view-only grants, so leads can list service accounts but not change them. `changeMemberRole` declares it too. 15 tests in `test_service_accounts.py` cover scopes, the workspace boundary, revocation on the next request, overlapping rotation, disabling, expiry, unknown tokens, hashes at rest, validation, unique names and the audit trail. The permission and isolation suites gained the five routes and two tables. Mutation checks ignored scopes, ignored revocation and dropped a write flag, and each was caught. Surprise: the first version of the permission test read the write flag from the same OpenAPI metadata it checked, so dropping the flag passed. It now treats every guarded call that is not a GET as a write.
- SEC-001 implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0028](adr/0028-data-classes-on-columns.md), accepted under the owner's standing approval, adds `secret` to the four classes the backlog named and declares a class on every column through SQLAlchemy's `info`. Today's 62 columns are 13 personal, 6 secret and 43 operational. `pnpm data-map:doc` writes [reference/data-map.md](reference/data-map.md). `test_data_map.py` fails on an unclassified column, a table missing from the map or a stale page, and `test_data_classes.py` covers the finder and the shared mixins. A mutation check that dropped the class from `users.email` failed the test.
- SEC-003 implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0027](adr/0027-audit-log-hash-chain-in-postgres.md), accepted under the owner's standing approval, puts the audit log in Postgres as `audit_events` (migration `0006`), written in the same transaction as the change. A trigger chains each workspace's entries with SHA-256 under an advisory lock, and the app role cannot update, delete or truncate them. `parfocal_api.audit.record` now records new organisations and workspaces, suspension and reactivation, members added, role changes with the old and new role, sign-in and sign-out. `GET /api/tenant/audit-events` (`audit-log` permission) exports a workspace's trail, optionally for one target, and `chain_breaks` verifies it outside the database. 9 tests in `test_audit.py` at first cover the chain across platform and member actions, the app role refused, the owner refused while the trigger stands, a changed and a removed entry found, 20 concurrent writers keeping one unbroken chain, a case's history in order and the read permission. The isolation and permission suites gained the table and the route. Mutation checks removed the advisory lock, the revoke, the append-only trigger and a field from the Python hash, and each was caught by the test meant for it. Surprise: `jsonb` rewrites its text, so details are stored as canonical JSON text to keep the hash reproducible. A side review pointed out that an unchangeable log holding IdP subjects and user ids would block GDPR erasure, so before the first commit people became pseudonyms in a separate erasable `audit_people` table, and `forget` removes the link without breaking the chain. 4 more tests cover entries holding no subjects, forgetting keeping every hash, only the platform forgetting, and one workspace not resolving another's people, which a mutation of that policy showed to be needed.

## 2026-10-13

- IAM-006 done after CI run 38420215082 passed on `main`.
- IAM-006 implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. [ADR 0026](adr/0026-roles-and-permission-matrix.md), accepted under the owner's standing approval, replaces the nine planning roles with the UX kit's six and its 26-row matrix. `parfocal_api.permissions` holds the matrix letter for letter (checked against the kit's `PERMS` array), memberships carry a role (migration `0005`), `authenticate` puts it in `MemberPrincipal`, and routes declare `require(Permission.X)`, which also writes `x-permission` into the OpenAPI schema. `listTenantMembers` needs `see-team`, and the new `PUT /api/tenant/members/{user_id}/role` needs `manage-people` and refuses changing one's own role. `docs/reference/permissions.md` is generated by `pnpm permissions:doc`. `test_permissions.py` checks the matrix, the page, that every operation declares a permission or a reason to be open, and calls every guarded route as all six roles. A mutation check showed that a flipped cell fails the page test and a route without its guard fails the coverage test. Surprise: Alembic applies the naming convention to `drop_constraint` too, so it takes the short name.
- IAM-005 done after CI run 38419319746 passed on `main`, including the `e2e` job.
- IAM-005 implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. Migration `0004` adds `users` (IdP subject, email, display name, initials, an avatar colour index from 0 to 11 for DS-014 to map, title), tenant-owned `memberships` and the session's active workspace. The database scope gained `app.user_id`, so a person sees their own memberships and those workspaces across tenants, members of a tenant see each other's profiles, and nothing else. Login creates or updates the user and reopens the last workspace, or the only one. `/api/me` reads and edits the profile and lists workspaces, `PUT /api/me/workspace` switches after checking membership and suspension, `/api/tenant/members` lists colleagues, and operators add and list members under `/api/platform/tenants/{id}/members`. 17 API tests in `test_members.py` cover two memberships and switching, refusals, the remembered workspace, losing a membership, profile edits and validation, and the operator routes. The isolation suite now seeds a member per tenant and checks the new routes. Surprise: the users policy reads `memberships`, so the downgrade must drop that policy before the table.
- IAM-002, IAM-003 and IAM-004 done after CI run 38418493357 passed on `main`, including the new `e2e` job, which logged the test user in through Zitadel from a GitHub runner in 20 s.
- IAM-002 implemented, not ticked until CI is green on `main`. `parfocal_api.auth` runs the code flow with PKCE against Zitadel through a small client on `httpx2` and PyJWT (`parfocal_api.oidc`), stores pending logins and sessions in Postgres by hash with tokens sealed under `SESSION_KEY`, refreshes server-side when the access token expires, and revokes and ends the Zitadel session on logout. Migration `0003` adds `auth_logins`, `sessions` and `platform_operators` under a new auth-only scope. `authenticate` now returns a signed-in user or a platform operator, and `python -m parfocal_api.operators` manages operators. 26 API tests in `test_auth.py` use a fake identity provider that signs real tokens. They cover the cookie flags, open-redirect attempts, missing binding cookies, replayed states, tampered and foreign-signed ID tokens, refresh, refused refresh, logout, expired and forged sessions, secrets at rest and operators. `apps/e2e` holds the first Playwright tests, which log the test user in through real Zitadel to a deep link, force a server-side refresh and log out. They passed three runs in a row locally (`pnpm test:e2e`). At the owner's request the dev app's client ID and secret and the test user's name and password were added as GitHub repository secrets, and a new `e2e` CI job runs the tests with traces off, because traces record the typed password. Vite's dev server now proxies `/api` to `pnpm api:dev`. Surprises: after logout Zitadel offers the remembered account and only then asks for the password, and the redirect-style routes generated `void` types that Oxlint flagged, so they are left out of the OpenAPI schema. See [knowledge/auth.md](knowledge/auth.md).
- STACK-028 done. At the owner's request Zitadel was used again. A scratch backend on `localhost:5173` logged the new test user `e2e-pathologist@parfocal.eu` in through Zitadel Cloud with PKCE, refreshed server-side, revoked on logout and read the organisation claims. [ADR 0025](adr/0025-zitadel-bff-login.md) was accepted under the owner's standing approval. Numbers are in [results/2026-10-13-zitadel-bff-login-spike.md](results/2026-10-13-zitadel-bff-login-spike.md). Surprise: a SameSite=Strict session cookie set on the callback is not sent on the page a 302 lands on, not even after a reload, so the callback answers with an HTML hop. IdP organisations map to ours through the resource-owner claim when per-organisation SSO arrives (IAM-012). Until then everyone lives in the `Parfocal` Zitadel organisation and our database decides memberships.
- IAM-003 implemented, not ticked until CI runs it on `main`. [ADR 0024](adr/0024-workspaces-are-tenants.md) makes each workspace a tenant grouped by an organisation, accepted under the owner's standing approval after they asked for the best option that is not too complicated. Migration `0002` adds `organisations` and `tenants` (slug, kind, region, plan, status), both with forced row-level security that lets members read only their own rows and only the platform scope write. `parfocal_api.tenants` serves `/api/platform/organisations`, `/api/platform/tenants` with get, suspend and reactivate for platform operators, and `/api/tenant` for members. A suspended tenant's requests get a 403 `tenant-suspended` problem. 18 API tests in `test_tenants.py` cover creation, several workspaces per organisation, slug conflicts, invalid drafts, suspension, reactivation, members kept off platform routes and operators kept out of tenant routes. The client was regenerated. IAM-002, IAM-005 and IAM-006 were edited to match.
- IAM-004 implemented, not ticked until CI runs it on `main`. `scoped_transaction` in `parfocal_common.db` sets the tenant, guest case and view and platform settings in one statement, and `parfocal_api.context` is the only place that turns a principal into that scope. `authenticate` refuses everything until IAM-002. `test_tenant_isolation.py` runs every tenant GET route as tenant A and tenant B through the pooler, refuses members on platform routes, and fails when a table has no seeder or a GET operation is not registered. Further tests cover forced row-level security on every table, models matching migrations, a guest without a grant getting no scope, and settings not lingering on a pooled connection. A mutation check removed `getCurrentTenant`'s filter (still isolated by row-level security) and then loosened the policy (the suite failed). Surprise: FastAPI's default dependency scope commits after the response is sent, so the connections use `scope="function"`, see [knowledge/api.md](knowledge/api.md). The pooler gained a `*` database entry so tests can use a fresh database.

State is encrypted and stored in the `parfocal-tfstate` R2 bucket. The first apply ran (1 imported, 10 added) and the follow-up plan is empty. Surprise: the Zitadel provider wanted to replace every app on each plan until `org_id` and the app defaults were set explicitly. DNS records, Neon and the policy check are still missing for OPS-001. See [knowledge/infra.md](knowledge/infra.md).
- FOUND-002 and FOUND-003 done. The agent guide and docs memory system are committed and linked from the README. `scripts/check_adrs.py` fails when an ADR lacks a valid `- Status:` line, names a missing successor or is not listed in [adr/README.md](adr/README.md), with six unit tests in `scripts/test_check_adrs.py`. The `docs` workflow in `.github/workflows/docs.yml` runs both on every pull request and push to `main`, and its first run passed (GitHub Actions run 37531536582). Pushing workflow files needed the `workflow` scope on the `gh` token.
- Renamed the project from the working name pathviewer to Parfocal in the docs, bucket names and repository path, and linked the repository to `fedasevich/parfocal`. Provisioned the accounts listed in [knowledge/agent-tooling.md](knowledge/agent-tooling.md): the `parfocal.eu` zone and `parfocal-dev` bucket on Cloudflare, the Neon project through Vercel, the Zitadel Cloud instance in the EU and the Modal `dev` and `staging` environments. Workers Paid is postponed. Entries below that say `pathviewer-dev` mean `parfocal-dev`.
- Recorded the product name Parfocal and the domain `parfocal.eu` in [project/overview.md](project/overview.md). Started account setup for Cloudflare, Neon, Modal and Zitadel Cloud.
- Decided in [ADR 0005](adr/0005-external-sends-only-in-production.md) that only production sends email, errors, telemetry and analytics. One `APP_ENV` setting picks the clients at startup and every other environment logs to the console, with per-concern overrides for tests and drills. Edited FOUND-009, FOUND-010, STACK-032, STACK-033, STACK-036, SEC-010, OPS-006 and OPS-007 and updated [knowledge/local-dev.md](knowledge/local-dev.md).
- Switched development from local emulators to free cloud resources in [ADR 0004](adr/0004-cloud-dev-resources.md), after checking every service's free plan. Development uses a `pathviewer-dev` R2 bucket, real Resend with test recipients and the Zitadel Cloud instance. Sentry, Grafana and PostHog stay off locally. Docker Postgres is the only local service, because queries from a local API to Neon over the internet would be too slow. MinIO, Mailpit, local Zitadel, Spotlight and `grafana/otel-lgtm` are gone. Edited FOUND-009, STACK-032 and STACK-036 and rewrote [knowledge/local-dev.md](knowledge/local-dev.md). Surprises were Resend's shared 100-a-day cap and Modal Starter's limit on crons and web endpoints.
- Settled the platform architecture in an interview and recorded it as [ADR 0003](adr/0003-pilot-platform-architecture.md). TypeScript runs on Cloudflare (Pages, a one-origin Worker router, Durable Objects, R2), Python runs on Modal and the database is Neon. Login goes through Zitadel Cloud with a BFF cookie in FastAPI, edge access uses capability tokens, tenancy uses RLS, jobs use Postgres tables driven by Modal and realtime uses Durable Objects as ephemeral fan-out. The pilot holds only public slides with fake identities, so regions are not pinned. Edited the defaults of 14 STACK slots and the FOUND, IAM, TILES, COLLAB, AIP, SEC, OPS and TEST tasks that assumed Kubernetes, Temporal or Valkey, and added a "Pilot data" row to the baseline. Surprises were that Modal region pinning needs the $250 a month Team plan plus a 1.5x multiplier, that R2 presigned GETs bypass the CDN, and that the Workers free plan cannot carry tile traffic. Added [knowledge/platform.md](knowledge/platform.md), [knowledge/local-dev.md](knowledge/local-dev.md), [knowledge/agent-tooling.md](knowledge/agent-tooling.md), risks R8 to R10 and a project `.mcp.json` with the official remote MCP servers for Cloudflare, Neon, Sentry, PostHog and Resend. No STACK task is ticked, because each still needs its spike.
- Replaced relative POC references with full links. Code references in the backlog now link to the private GitHub repo, and [project/poc-reference.md](project/poc-reference.md) lists every POC doc with GitHub and local paths plus the clone command.
- Added a `.gitignore` for the TypeScript and Python monorepo (Node, Vite, Turborepo, Playwright, uv and Python caches, OpenTofu state, secrets, slide files and model weights) and renamed the default branch to `main`.
- Set up the docs memory system, the shared agent guide (`AGENTS.md`, imported by `CLAUDE.md`), ADR and result templates, and the project overview, roadmap and risk register. FOUND-002 and the template half of FOUND-003 are written but stay unticked until committed and, for FOUND-003, until the CI check exists. See [ADR 0001](adr/0001-docs-as-project-memory.md).
- Wrote the full backlog from the UX kit, the hi-fi mock and the POC docs after a planning session. 520 tasks in 37 epics, milestones M0 to M6. See [BACKLOG.md](BACKLOG.md) and [ADR 0002](adr/0002-planning-baseline.md).
